# Bevy Mesh, Core Pipeline, Sprite, and Image — Epix Match Status

Reference: Bevy tag `v0.18.0` (commit
`5f8270f2e049f90139a503d1e930070d926f9427`) in `tmp/bevy-0.18.0`, compared
with `epix_engine/mesh`, `epix_engine/render/core_graph`,
`epix_engine/sprite`, `epix_engine/sprite_render`, and `epix_engine/image` (C++23 no-modules
build). The sprite audit covers both
Bevy's `bevy_sprite` and `bevy_sprite_render` crates.

This is the source-of-truth tracker for the mesh/core-graph parity effort.
Every row is retained until it is fixed or the user explicitly accepts it as
intentional, deferred, or out of scope.

## Legend

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| ✅| Functionally equivalent |
| ⚠️ | Intentional divergence; rationale recorded |
| ❌| Present but behavior or API differs; must fix |
| 🚫 | Missing; must implement |
| REVIEW | Audit incomplete; not yet a parity claim |
| N/A | Non-implementable or out of scope, only with user confirmation |

## Translation Rules

| Bevy | Epix |
| --- | --- |
| `Option<T>` | `std::optional<T>` / optional reference wrapper for borrowing |
| `Result<T, E>` | `std::expected<T, E>` |
| ECS resource and system | Epix resource and scheduled system |
| Rust iterator/slice | C++ range/`std::span` view |
| `wgpu` resource wrapper | Direct `wgpu::` resource where that is an accepted Epix convention |

## Mesh Render Asset and Allocator

| ID | Bevy entity / behavior | Epix counterpart | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | `MeshRenderAssetPlugin`: registers render asset + allocator and initializes vertex layouts | `render::mesh::MeshRenderAssetPlugin` | ✅| The render-owned plugin composes `RenderAssetPlugin<Mesh>` and `MeshAllocatorPlugin`, then initializes render-world `MeshVertexBufferLayouts`. Mesh2d rendering consumes it from the separate sprite-render module instead of owning allocator state. |
| M2 | `RenderMesh`: CPU render metadata, vertex-layout reference, indexed buffer metadata | `RenderMesh` (replaces `GPUMesh`) | ✅| `GPUMesh` is removed; `RenderMesh` (`vertex_count`, `RenderMeshBufferInfo`, `MeshVertexBufferLayoutRef`, primitive topology) is the render asset the `RenderAsset<RenderMesh>` specialization produces. Mesh2d draw commands bind the vertex/index buffers from `MeshAllocator::mesh_vertex_slice`/`mesh_index_slice` with the interleaved layout's stride/offsets, and the pipeline cache specializes per interned layout (Bevy Mesh2dPipelineKey's layout id). Verified: 27 mesh + 189 render + text tests, plus client-area captures of mesh_rendering (1280x720, center (174,195,189) —identical to the pre-rework baseline), alpha_mask ((253,160,79)), batching ((42,42,46)), and font_image —all unchanged. |
| M3 | `RenderMeshBufferInfo` | `RenderMeshBufferInfo` | ✅| The `Indexed { count, index_format }` / `NonIndexed` discriminator now drives the draw (`indexed()`); index format comes from the metadata, and the index buffer+range comes from `mesh_index_slice`. Non-indexed geometry draws `0..vertex_count` from the vertex slice in both mesh and text draw commands. |
| M4 | `RenderAsset<RenderMesh>`: fallible prepare with previous GPU asset and render-asset usage | `RenderAsset<RenderMesh>` (`SourceAsset = Mesh`) | ✅| **Re-keyed to Bevy's shape (2026-10).** The specialization is keyed by the *render* asset, with `SourceAsset` naming the main-world asset; `prepare_asset` returns `T` and takes `const T* previous`; `usage` is renamed `asset_usage(const SourceAsset&)`. `ProcessedAsset` is gone — the template argument *is* the processed type. Epix additionally keeps `ExtractedAsset` next to `SourceAsset`, which is its deliberate improvement over Bevy: today the two coincide for every specialization, but a compact extraction stays expressible without another structural change. `Mesh`'s public `asset_usage` is preserved through `take_gpu_data` and returned by `asset_usage`. The re-key rippled through all four specializations (`RenderMesh`, `GpuImage`, `GpuShaderStorageBuffer`, `PreparedMaterial2d<M>`, declarations and definitions), the `RenderAssets<…>` call sites (37 sites / 18 files) and `ExtractedAssets<…>` (4 sites), which are keyed by the same render type. Verified by build; CTest and captures pending with the mesh2d/material2d batch. |
| M5 | `MeshAllocatorPlugin` and `MeshAllocator`: slab allocation, update, free, buffer slices | `MeshAllocatorPlugin` + `MeshAllocator` | ✅| The public `allocate_and_free_meshes` system now frees removed/modified allocations, allocates every extracted mesh, grows each affected general slab once, then uploads every payload, matching Bevy's three-phase frame flow. `MeshAllocator` is a move-only ready/finish-time resource with public `extra_buffer_usages` and query methods; implementation state and helper types are private/detail-only. General/large buffer usages, pending/resident transitions, actual-buffer `slabs_size()`, slot-capacity rounding, slab-ID sentinel skipping, and first-growth-capacity retention match Bevy. `general_vertex_slabs_supported` is retained; while wgpu-native's C API lacks Bevy's downlevel-flags query, Epix's temporary Vulkan coercion guarantees `BASE_VERTEX`, so `from_world` records true with a colocated removal note. |
| M6 | `MeshAllocatorSettings` | `MeshAllocatorSettings` | ✅| Settings are a separate render-world resource initialized by `MeshAllocatorPlugin`, preserved when configured before plugin attachment, and passed explicitly into allocation. `MeshAllocator` no longer owns settings, matching Bevy's resource/API boundary. |
| M7 | `MeshBufferSlice`, `SlabId`, allocation query methods | `render::mesh::{SlabId, MeshBufferSlice}`, `MeshAllocator` query methods | ✅| The generic allocator value types and allocator implementation both live at the Bevy-shaped render-layer boundary. `MeshBufferSlice` exposes one element `range`, and the real offset-allocator-backed query methods (`mesh_vertex_slice`, `mesh_index_slice`, `mesh_slabs`, `slab_count`, `slabs_size`, `allocations`) are consumed by mesh/text draw commands. |
| M8 | Optional morph-target image/data, mesh extraction, and render inheritance/preparation | detailed by M8a–M8e | REVIEW | M8a–M8d are implemented, verified, and approved. The source audit showed this is cross-module: `bevy_mesh` owns the data/API and scheduling set, while `bevy_render::mesh` owns `MorphPlugin`, direct-child inheritance, render-asset retry, texture-view retention, and the base pipeline bit. The previous N12/M8 wording incorrectly made all of it sound mesh-owned. M8e remains open because visible vertex deformation belongs to Bevy PBR and Epix does not yet have the corresponding PBR render path. |
| M8a | `MorphAttributes`, `MorphTargetImage`, `MorphBuildError`, `MAX_MORPH_WEIGHTS`, and `lowest_2d` packing | implementation in `epix::mesh` backed by `epix::image::Image` | ✅ | Ports Bevy's 256-target and 2048x2048-layer limits, exact error variants/messages, smallest-padding rectangle search, R32Float 3D texture shape, position/normal/tangent component order, per-layer padding, and asset usage. C++ uses fallible `create(...)` because `new` is a language keyword; its input remains a sized outer range of arbitrary inner input ranges. |
| M8b | `MorphWeights`, `MeshMorphWeights`, and all morph fields/accessors on `Mesh` | implementation in `epix::mesh` | ✅ | Ports canonical-node and primitive weights, first-mesh metadata, mutable slices, clear/extend, target image/name states, fallible/infallible accessors and consuming builders. Copy, equality, render-world extraction, and `SerializedMesh`'s intentional omission all include the new fields. |
| M8c | `InheritWeightSystems`, render-owned `MorphPlugin`, and changed-parent direct-child `inherit_weights` | mesh scheduling label plus `render::mesh` plugin/system | ✅ | The parent query excludes `Mesh3d`, runs only for changed `MorphWeights`, and overwrites only direct child entities that have both `Mesh3d` and `MeshMorphWeights`, matching pinned `bevy_render/src/mesh/mod.rs`. Epix explicitly configures the set because its scheduler warns on Bevy's implicit set-label form. The regression covers direct-child scope, both exclusions, changed-parent propagation, and unchanged-parent preservation. |
| M8d | `RenderMesh::morph_targets`; mesh preparation waits for `GpuImage`, retains its view, and sets `BaseMeshPipelineKey::MORPH_TARGETS` | implementation in `epix_render` | ✅ | Missing target images return the existing `RetryNextAssetUpdate<Mesh>` payload so preparation retries without losing the extracted mesh. The texture view and flag are retained only after the target image is GPU-ready. The parent render-module change was explicitly reviewed and approved before commit. |
| M8e | PBR per-entity weight extraction, double-buffered uniforms, no-automatic-batching marker, bind-group layouts/entries, and vertex deformation shader | none; Epix PBR currently contains pipeline-key value types only | REVIEW | Pinned Bevy's visible morph deformation is implemented by `bevy_pbr`, not by mesh/image/render. Epix has no PBR mesh pipeline, bind groups, standard material, or shader path to extend, so a visual morph example cannot honestly be supplied by this batch. This dependency is recorded, not silently treated as complete or accepted out of scope. |
| M9 | `MeshExtractableData` and `MeshAccessError`: distinguish data, absent data, and data already extracted to the render world | `MeshExtractableData<T>` with `Data`, `NoData`, and `ExtractedToRenderWorld` states | ✅| Mesh attributes and indices now preserve Bevy's three distinct states. A newly constructed empty mesh extracts successfully exactly once; later access reports `ExtractedToRenderWorld`, while absent indices report `NotFound`. Public fallible/infallible attribute and index accessors, lazy attribute ranges, insertion/removal builders, cloning, and render-asset error mapping follow Bevy's contract. |
| M10 | Built-in `MeshVertexAttribute` IDs and raw packed-layout shader locations | `Mesh::ATTRIBUTE_*` slots and `Mesh::get_mesh_vertex_buffer_layout` | ✅| Built-in IDs/names/formats and `FIRST_AVAILABLE_CUSTOM_ATTRIBUTE=8` match Bevy. Attribute names are borrowed `std::string_view` data, full attributes convert to IDs, raw packed layouts assign iteration-index shader locations, and `MeshVertexBufferLayout::get_layout` remaps stable IDs to each pipeline's requested shader locations. Mesh2d uses Bevy's position=0, normal=1, uv=2, tangent=3, color=4 interface; text uses custom ID 8 remapped to location 1. Missing attributes return `MissingVertexAttributeError`. |
| M11 | `calculate_bounds_2d`: separate new-mesh insertion from changed-mesh updates | `calculate_bounds_2d` | ✅| Added Bevy-style `AsAssetId` and `AssetChanged<T>` support in the owning assets module, with change ticks maintained while asset events are flushed. The C++ customization point is the `AsAssetId<T>` template struct and its validating concept is `AsAssetIdImpl`. Bounds are inserted only for meshes missing `Aabb` and updated only when the `Mesh2d` component or referenced mesh asset changes, with Bevy's `NoFrustumCulling`/`NoAutoAabb` exclusions. A render-only mesh is no longer read again after its CPU data has transferred. |
| M12 | `MeshVertexBufferLayouts` structural hash interning and pointer-hashable `MeshVertexBufferLayoutRef` | `MeshVertexBufferLayouts` hash set and pointer-hashable `MeshVertexBufferLayoutRef` | ✅| The store uses heterogeneous structural hashing/equality to intern layouts with average-constant-time lookup, while returned references compare and hash by their shared pointee address. Structurally equal layouts therefore share one identity exactly as in Bevy. |
| M13 | `BaseMeshPipelineKey` high-bit topology encoding and morph flag | `BaseMeshPipelineKey` | ✅ | The key shape and topology helpers were already present. M8d now exercises the remaining behavior by setting `MORPH_TARGETS` during render-asset preparation only after the referenced morph image is GPU-ready. |
| M14 | `Mesh2d`, `Mesh3d`, `MeshTag`, `AsAssetId` conversions, and `mark_3d_meshes_as_changed_if_their_assets_changed` | matched mesh components and PostUpdate system | ✅| Both mesh newtypes default to an invalid weak handle, dereference to their handle, convert to `AssetId<Mesh>`, implement `AsAssetId`, compare by value, and require `Transform`. `MeshTag` defaults to zero. `MeshPlugin` schedules the public propagation system after asset-event writing; exactly like Bevy, it responds only to `Modified` events and marks only referencing `Mesh3d` components changed. Bevy handles `Mesh2d` asset changes separately through `AssetChanged<Mesh2d>` specialization invalidation while rebuilding extracted instances each frame; matching that Epix render-side condition remains tracked under S9. |
| M15 | Camera-owned `MeshAabb for Mesh`; dependency direction is camera →mesh | `camera::MeshAabb<mesh::Mesh>` specialization; camera →mesh | ✅| The mesh member was removed. Camera owns the specialization and implementation, directly includes/imports mesh, and mesh no longer depends on camera. No cross-module forward declaration is used. The implementation and transformed-AABB half-space calculation follow Bevy's componentwise-absolute transform logic. Landed 2026-09-13: `compute_aabb` now opens with Bevy's `Mesh::final_aabb` short-circuit and maps every access failure to "no bounds" (`let Ok(..) = .. else { return None }`), so an extracted mesh keeps its bounds instead of making the previous throw, which was an Epix invention; the camera parity test covers both the precomputed-extents path and the no-bounds paths. |
| M16 | Full `Mesh` API: topology, attributes, indices, duplicate/merge, transforms, winding, normals/tangents, joints, triangles, asset IDs, extraction state, constants, morph state, and final AABB | partial `Mesh` API; only `generate_tangents` (M22) remains | REVIEW | Core extraction/access behavior is covered by M4/M9/M10. M16/M21 cover duplicate/merge, transforms, winding, normal generation, joint-weight normalization, triangle iteration, asset IDs, equality/serialization, extraction state, constants, and final AABB with direct pinned-Bevy regression cases. M8b now adds morph target image/name state, fallible and infallible accessors, consuming builders, copying, equality, extraction, and the intentional serialized-transfer omission. Still unmatched: `generate_tangents` and `GenerateTangentsError` (M22, gated on MikkTSpace availability). |
| M16a | `Mesh` value equality (`PartialEq`) across topology, payload states, usage, final AABB, and optional morph metadata | defaulted `Mesh::operator==` over Bevy-aligned value members | ✅ | Existing equality covered descriptor/value maps, absent/present/extracted index and attribute states, topology, `asset_usage`, and `final_aabb`. M8b extends the same defaulted comparison to morph target handle/name states; raytracing remains governed by M16-D2. |
| M16-D1 | Bevy's `assert!` preconditions in the mesh algorithms (a `TriangleList` topology, the presence or absence of an index buffer, a `float3` position attribute) become panics. | The ported algorithms use `<cassert>` `assert`s, exactly as the module's pre-existing `scale_by`/`transform_by` guards do. | ⚠️ | The `msvc-reldeb-no-modules` preset compiles with `NDEBUG`, so in the verified build these checks are compiled out and invalid input is processed instead of panicking. Recorded rather than silently changed, because the core-graph rows (C13-D10) chose a thrown typed error for the same situation, so the two conventions now differ. Closing it is one decision applied across the mesh algorithm surface (throw, or a project-level `EPIX_ASSERT`), not a per-call-site fix. |
| M16-D2 | `Mesh::enable_raytracing: bool` (Bevy `mesh.rs:256`, defaulted `true` by `Mesh::new`) and its `bevy_solari` BLAS path | No such field. | ⚠️ | Out of scope rather than deferred: the field's only reader is `bevy_solari` (`scene/blas.rs:182`), and Epix has no raytracing/BLAS subsystem to honour it. Recorded so the missing field is a stated divergence instead of an unnoticed one, and because it sits between `asset_usage` and `final_aabb` in Bevy's struct. |
| M17 | `Indices`, `MeshWindingInvertError`, `MeshTrianglesError`, iteration/range conversion, byte views, swap/remove, and winding operations | detailed by M17a–M17d | ✅| The complete source-level sub-audit is covered by the four rows below. |
| M17a | `Indices`: U16/U32 alternatives, lazy `iter`, `len`, `is_empty`, `push`/`Extend` promotion, and `IndexFormat` conversion | replacing type-erased `MeshIndices` with `Indices` | ✅| The variant-backed type preserves the active width, returns a lazy C++ range, promotes U16 storage on the first value above `u16::MAX` without losing the remainder, and exposes exact Bevy names and format conversion. `Mesh::get_index_buffer_bytes` and all production consumers use the new contract. |
| M17b | `MeshWindingInvertError` and `MeshTrianglesError` variants, nested `MeshAccessError`, and display text | tagged C++ variants with matching alternatives and messages | ✅| Rust data enums map to `std::variant`; payloadless variants are distinct marker structs and `MeshAccessError` is retained as the payload-bearing alternative. |
| M17c | `Mesh::duplicate_vertices`, winding inversion, and consuming builder variants | `mesh_algorithms` | ✅| Matches Bevy's indexed attribute expansion, index removal, fallible/infallible and consuming APIs, per-topology winding behavior, and typed errors. |
| M17d | `MeshTrianglesError` and lazy `Mesh::triangles` across indexed triangle lists/strips, including missing-data and invalid-face behavior | `MeshTriangles` standard view; `mesh_algorithms` | ✅| The expected lazy range requires indices, truncates incomplete triangle lists, alternates triangle-strip winding, and silently omits faces containing out-of-range indices. Validation order and extracted-data errors match Bevy; its `BadIndices` alternative remains present but is not emitted by this method. |
| M18 | Full `VertexAttributeValues` variant set, conversions/errors, length/emptiness, typed access, raw bytes, and supported-format mapping | detailed by M18a–M18c | ✅| All value, conversion, `Mesh` storage, algorithm, camera-bounds, and render-packing paths now preserve Bevy's semantic vertex-format identity. |
| M18a | All 28 `VertexAttributeValues` alternatives; variant metadata, equality, `len`/`is_empty`, `as_float3`, `get_bytes`, and `VertexFormat` mapping | standalone `VertexAttributeValues`; `Mesh` migration remains M18c | ✅| Every Bevy 0.18 alternative has distinct semantic identity even where C++ element types coincide. Metadata, byte views, and the complete WebGPU vertex-format size table have paired header/module-source tests. |
| M18b | Bevy conversion matrix for scalar/array and math-vector inputs/outputs plus owning `FromVertexAttributeError` | constructors and rvalue `try_into<T>()` | ✅| Covers all Bevy scalar/array and math-vector conversions, including both integer and normalized small-integer alternatives, and preserves the rejected value in errors. `glm::vec3` represents both Bevy `Vec3` and `Vec3A`, an intentional math-library adaptation rather than a semantic omission. |
| M18c | Internal `MeshAttributeData` and every `Mesh` attribute accessor/mutator/algorithm use `VertexAttributeValues`; render packing consumes its bytes | public `MeshAttributeData`/`AttributeMap` backed by `ecs::untyped_vector` | ✅| The storage pair and map are implementation details as in Bevy. Insertion now rejects semantic format mismatches rather than accepting equal byte widths; access/removal expose tagged values, triangle and bounds readers use `as_float3`, duplication preserves every tag, and interleaving consumes descriptor-sized chunks from the tagged bytes. The consuming insertion builder is rvalue-qualified and returns by value, matching Bevy while avoiding a dangling temporary. Cross-module implementation was verified and committed after the required parent-module review. |
| M19 | `Meshable`/`MeshBuilder`, complete 2D and 3D primitive builders, extrusion/rings, UV modes, anchors, and builder errors | detailed by M19a–M19f | ✅ | **Complete:** every sub-row is matched — the 2D families (M19a/M19b), the three 3D batches (M19c/M19d), the sphere and icosphere (M19e) and the extrusion path (M19f). The source-level sub-audit and every row's evidence live in `documentation/mesh-primitive-builders-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` (B1–B49). |
| M19a | `MeshBuilder`/`Meshable`; `Circle`, `Ellipse`, `RegularPolygon`, and `Rectangle` primitives and builders | matched in `epix::mesh` | ✅| Mesh-facing defaults and constructors, generic `Mesh` conversion, vertex order, +Z normals, UVs, U32 indices, and default asset usage match Bevy 0.18. Epix has no standalone math-primitives module, so the minimal shape inputs live with their mesh builders; unrelated `bevy_math` measurement/query APIs are outside this mesh row. C++ cannot have a `resolution` field and same-named method; the public field is retained and the consuming method is named `with_resolution`. |
| M19b | Remaining standalone 2D sector, segment, polygon, polyline, annulus, rhombus, triangle, and capsule builders | completed by M19b1–M19b3 plus `Ring` | ✅| Circular sectors/segments (M19b1), the convex-polygon/segment/polyline builders (M19b2), and the annulus/rhombus/triangle/capsule builders (M19b3) are all implemented and verified. `Ring<P>`/`RingMeshBuilder<P>` closed with them (B24). Generic rings were listed here with their `Extrudable` dependency, which is now only M19f. |
| M19b1 | `CircularMeshUvMode`, `CircularSectorMeshBuilder`, `CircularSegmentMeshBuilder`, and shape conversions | matched in `epix::mesh` | ✅| Defaults, +Y-symmetric arc/chord geometry, center/chord fan, +Z normals, mask UV rotation, U32 indices, and default asset usage match Bevy. `Arc2d` and minimal sector/segment inputs live in mesh under the M19a ownership adaptation. C++ fluent setters are `with_resolution`/`with_uv_mode` because fields and methods cannot share names. |
| M19b2 | `ConvexPolygonMeshBuilder`, `Segment2dMeshBuilder`, and `Polyline2dMeshBuilder` | matched in `epix::mesh` | ✅| B1–B15: index/attribute insertion order, `LineList` topology, `flat_map` pair order, `TriangleList` fan indices, `ConvexPolygon` validation and error text, and winding-order numerics all match. One real mismatch was found and fixed: Epix's convex-polygon builder emitted `ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL`/`ATTRIBUTE_UV_0` that Bevy's does not. |
| M19b3 | `AnnulusMeshBuilder`, `RhombusMeshBuilder`, `Triangle2dMeshBuilder`, and `Capsule2dMeshBuilder` | matched in `epix::mesh` | ✅| B16–B23: shape defaults and measurement helpers, annulus seam duplication with wrapping UVs, rhombus quadrant folding, triangle3d UV projection with the index-winding flip, and the capsule's half-step start plus `radius_frac` UV compression all match. |
| M19c | Basic 3D cuboid, plane, segments, polylines, triangle, and tetrahedron builders | matched in `epix::mesh` | ✅| B32–B38: `Cuboid`/`CuboidMeshBuilder`, `Plane3d`/`PlaneMeshBuilder`, `Segment3d`/`Polyline3d` and their builders, `Triangle3d`/`Triangle3dMeshBuilder`, and `Tetrahedron`/`TetrahedronMeshBuilder`, each with Bevy's face table, grid subdivision, shortest-arc rotation, and exact generated data. **Newly deferred by this batch:** B39 records that `Camera3d` still has no 3D draw pipeline for these meshes, so the `primitives_3d` example renders them through Core2D until the 3D material pipeline exists. |
| M19d | Rotational solids: cylinder, cone/frustum, capsule, torus; UV modes, anchors, and builder errors | cylinder, torus, cone, conical frustum, and capsule matched | ✅ | B40–B46 implement `Cylinder`/`CylinderAnchor`/`CylinderMeshBuilder`, `Torus`/`TorusMeshBuilder`, `Cone`/`ConeAnchor`/`ConeMeshBuilder`, and `ConicalFrustum`/`ConicalFrustumMeshBuilder`, each with Bevy's ring/seam layout, cap winding, polar or normalized UVs, and anchor offset. `Capsule3d`/`Capsule3dMeshBuilder` closed with B47, which also showed they are independent of B49's `Extrudable` perimeter abstraction (Bevy's capsule builder never mentions extrusion), so every rotational solid in this batch is matched — the earlier ❌ status was stale and is corrected here. |
| M19e | UV sphere and icosphere builders plus `IcosphereError` | `Sphere`, `SphereKind`, `IcosphereError`, `SphereMeshBuilder` with `ico`/`uv`, and `Measured3dImpl<Sphere>` | ✅ | **Complete:** B48a ported the shape (with `Measured3d`), the builder surface, the UV sphere and Bevy's `>= 80` guard; B48b ported the `hexasphere` 16.0.0 icosphere generator, so `SphereKind::Ico` and with it the default kind mesh. The generated points, UVs and indices are asserted against the real crate's own recorded dumps (subdivisions 0 and 1 in full, 2 through 5 by count), and the `sphere` example builds the default `Ico { 5 }` kind end to end. |
| M19f | Extrusion, perimeter-segment iteration, and `Extrudable` support | `Extrusion<P>`, `ExtrusionBuilder<P>`, `Meshable for Extrusion<P>`, `PerimeterSegment`, the `ExtrudableImpl`/`Extrudable` pair, the free `perimeter(builder)`, the free `vertices_per_layer`/`indices_per_segment` helpers, the `ExtrusionResolutionShape` concept and `Measured3dImpl<Extrusion<P>>` | ✅ | **Complete:** B49a ported the `Extrudable` trait, all twelve perimeters and `PerimeterSegment` with its two helpers; B49b ported `Extrusion<P>`, `ExtrusionBuilder<P>` with `new`/`with_segments`/`with_inner` and the four per-shape `resolution` methods, `Meshable for Extrusion<P>`, the whole `build`, and `Measured3d for Extrusion<P>`. Every generated vertex, normal, UV and index is asserted against Bevy's own bodies in `tests/header/module_test.cpp`, and the dedicated `extrusion` example renders four prisms. The two C++ adaptations are recorded on B49: `with_segments` (the field owns Bevy's name) and the `ExtrusionResolutionShape<P>` concept that reproduces Bevy's four-shape-only `resolution` method. With M19e closed there is no primitive row left. |
| M20 | `SkinnedMesh` and `SkinnedMeshInverseBindposes` asset/component support | `SkinnedMesh` and `SkinnedMeshInverseBindposes` in `epix::mesh` | ✅ | Ported from pinned Bevy v0.18.0 `bevy_mesh/src/skinning.rs`. `SkinnedMesh` has the same invalid-handle/empty-joints default, public inverse-bindpose handle and ordered entity list, and an `AsAssetId` specialization returning that handle's ID. `SkinnedMeshInverseBindposes` owns a non-default-constructible matrix sequence; `matrices`, indexing, size, and iteration are the C++ counterpart of Bevy's immutable slice `Deref`. `MeshPlugin` registers the inverse-bindpose asset alongside `Mesh`. Reflection derives and Bevy's `#[entities]` remapping annotation have no Epix counterparts; entity references remain explicit `ecs::Entity` values. |
| M21 | `SerializedMesh`, deserializer, `MeshMergeError`, merge/duplicate-by-indices behavior | matched `SerializedMesh`, `MeshDeserializer`, `Mesh::merge`, `MeshMergeError`, and `Mesh` value equality | ✅ | **Complete.** The merge half retains the previously verified topology-first validation, shared-attribute append behavior, pre-append vertex offset, U16/U32 index handling, and typed errors. The serialization half ports Bevy's version-local, deliberately lossy transfer representation: only topology, ordered attributes, and optional indices survive; `asset_usage` returns to both worlds, `final_aabb` is cleared, and—now explicitly covered by M8b tests—morph target images/names are omitted. `MeshDeserializer` starts with all eight built-ins, ignores unregistered custom attributes with a warning, validates serialized attributes by registered name plus ID, and—matching Bevy exactly—does not compare the serialized format before installing the registered descriptor. Epix uses its existing `zpp_bits` binary backend through `serialize_serialized_mesh` / `deserialize_serialized_mesh` rather than claiming byte-format compatibility with serde JSON; this is the project-serializer adaptation of Bevy's `Serialize`/`Deserialize` derives. |
| M22 | Optional MikkTSpace tangent generation and typed `GenerateTangentsError` | none | 🚫 | **Dependency audit 2026-09-14:** pinned Bevy gates this module and its `Mesh::generate_tangents` / `with_generated_tangents` methods on the `bevy_mikktspace` feature. Epix has no MikkTSpace source, package, target, or equivalent implementation anywhere in its CMake files or vendored libraries. The gap is therefore confirmed rather than assumed; closing it requires a deliberate third-party dependency integration while preserving Bevy's typed topology/missing-attribute/format/generation errors. |
| M23 | `MeshPlugin` asset registration, inverse-bindpose asset, reflection registration, and PostUpdate asset-change scheduling | mesh and inverse-bindpose asset registration plus the PostUpdate asset-change system | ⚠️ | **Re-verified 2026-10 against Bevy `bevy_mesh/src/lib.rs:56-64` and `components.rs:127-147`** (the row's earlier REVIEW text already named the only remaining difference). Epix's `MeshPlugin::attach` (`mesh/src/mesh.cpp:1091-1097`) matches Bevy's `build` item for item: `app_register_asset<Mesh>` and `<SkinnedMeshInverseBindposes>` for `init_asset::<Mesh>()`/`init_asset::<SkinnedMeshInverseBindposes>()`, and `mark_3d_meshes_as_changed_if_their_assets_changed` in `PostUpdate` `.after(AssetSystems::WriteEvents)`, which is Bevy's `.after(AssetEventSystems)`. The system body matches as well: it collects only `AssetEvent<Mesh>::Type::Modified` ids, returns early when that set is empty, and marks every `Mesh3d` whose handle is in it — Bevy's `mesh_3d.set_changed()`, expressed as `get_mut()`, because Epix deliberately exposes no `set_modified()`/`set_added()` on `Mut<T>` (`documentation/ecs/change-detection.md`). **Intentional divergence:** `register_asset_reflect::<Mesh>()` has no counterpart, since Epix has no Bevy-style reflection registry for assets to receive it (asset (de)serialization uses per-type `zpp_bits` member lists instead). |

## Sprite and Sprite Render

| ID | Bevy entity / behavior | Epix counterpart | Status | Notes |
