| S1 | Separate `SpritePlugin` (CPU sprite facilities) and `SpriteRenderPlugin` (render composition); render plugin installs `Mesh2dRenderPlugin`, color material, and tilemap plugins | separate `epix_sprite` and `epix_sprite_render` targets/modules with matching plugin roles | ✅| Physical ownership and the dependency DAG match Bevy: CPU sprite is below render; sprite and Mesh2d rendering are in the higher sprite-render module. `SpriteRenderPlugin` installs `Mesh2dRenderPlugin`, `ColorMaterialPlugin`, `TilemapChunkPlugin` and `TilemapChunkMaterialPlugin` through `app.add_plugins`, exactly Bevy's tuple (`bevy_sprite_render/src/lib.rs:71-76`); Epix's plugin registry skips an already-added type, so an application may still add any of them itself. Closed 2026-10 with S10 and S14. |
| S2 | `Sprite`: image handle, optional texture atlas, color, flips, `custom_size`, optional `rect`, `image_mode`; constructors, `compute_pixel_space_point`, required components, visibility-class hook, and `AsAssetId` | matching CPU `Sprite` contract | ✅| The bundle-era image/component split was removed. Fields, constructors, pixel-space mapping, required components, visibility class, and `AsAssetId` now follow Bevy and have direct tests. |
| S3 | `Anchor` component with nine constants, default center, and vector conversion | `Anchor` component with matching constants and center default | ✅| Required by `Sprite`; constants and AABB effects are tested. |
| S4 | `SpriteImageMode` and `SpriteScalingMode`, including scale/uses-slices helpers | matched | ✅| The six proportional scaling modes now apply Bevy's UV crop or fitted-quad transforms during sprite preparation. The dedicated texture-slice example shows all six modes together. |
| S5 | `BorderRect`, `TextureSlice`, `TextureSlicer`, `SliceScaleMode`, computed slices, and asset/change-driven slice systems | matched | ✅| `ComputedTextureSlices` is maintained from Sprite changes and Image events, extracted through a lazy range, and expanded into per-slice GPU instances. Unit coverage exercises both maintenance paths; the visual example covers nine-slice and tiling. |
| S6 | `calculate_bounds_2d` jointly handles new/changed Mesh2d and Sprite entities in `SpritePlugin` | joint Mesh2d/Sprite bounds system | ✅| Sprite image/rect/atlas/custom-size/anchor branches and Bevy's disjoint query exclusions are implemented; creation and changed-component updates are tested. |
| S7 | `Text2d`, text layout/update/bounds/shadow API in `bevy_sprite`, plus render extraction integration when text is enabled | text facilities live in `epix_text`; `Text2d` now requires `Anchor`/`Visibility`/`VisibilityClass` and registers the `Sprite` visibility class | ✅ | **Closed 2026-10 (commit `b3fc90c7`).** `Text2d` matches Bevy's component surface (`bevy_sprite/src/text2d.rs:82-96`): `Transform`, `TextColor`, `Visibility`, `VisibilityClass`, `Anchor`, plus the `on_add = add_visibility_class::<Sprite>` hook that makes the sprite queue pick the glyph sprites up without a `Sprite` component, and `calculate_bounds_text2d` inserts the block AABB with Bevy's formula (`text2d.rs:316-347`). Text layout/shaping lives in `epix_text` (Epix's split of `bevy_text`, with FreeType/HarfBuzz instead of cosmic-text/swash) while the Bevy-shaped `TextLayoutInfo` data (`ShapedText`: per-glyph `position`/`size`/`atlas_info` plus the rounded advance box) and the atlas types live beside it, exactly as `bevy_text` owns them. Divergences that stay intentional are recorded in the audit log: no `Text2dShadow`, no spans, no `scale_factor`, and no `LineHeight` component. |
| S8 | Optional sprite picking backend, settings, camera marker/mode, hit testing, and plugin | none | 🚫 | Missing. Feature gating/module integration must be assessed against Epix's picking support. |
| S9 | `Mesh2dRenderPlugin`, view specialization caches/ticks, batched instance buffer, exact extracted instance model, bind groups, and draw commands | custom `MeshRenderPlugin` / simplified instance pipeline | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: unchanged in substance, but the scope is larger than the row implied.** Bevy's `Mesh2dRenderPlugin` surface (`extract_mesh2d`, `Mesh2dPipeline`/`Mesh2dPipelineKey`, `Mesh2dViewBindGroup`, the per-view key cache and specialization tick pair, and the `Opaque2d`/`AlphaMask2d`/`Transparent2d` draw commands) is still absent; Epix renders Mesh2d through the hand-rolled `MeshRenderPlugin` path. Its fate is bound to S10: Bevy's view-key cache and tick pair exist only to answer `Material2d` queueing, so they are ported together with S10d rather than added as unused resources. **Scope finding:** the port is a wholesale *replacement*, not an addition. `sprite_render/src/mesh2d.cpp` is 891 lines built around **eight embedded hand-written Slang shader variants** (solid / vertex-colour / textured / textured-vertex-colour vertices crossed with colour and mask fragments) with a local `Mesh2dPipeline` struct, whereas Bevy has one embedded `mesh2d.wgsl` vertex/fragment pair driven by `Mesh2dPipelineKey` with the material supplying the fragment shader. Porting `Mesh2dPipeline`, `Mesh2dPipelineKey`, `init_mesh_2d_pipeline`, `Mesh2dViewBindGroup`/`prepare_mesh2d_view_bind_groups`, the three mesh2d draw commands and `extract_mesh2d` therefore retires that whole design and must land with S10c/S10d rather than beside it. Physical ownership is corrected to sprite-render, but public names, extraction shape, shader-visible uniforms, scheduling, and batching remain different. Bevy's `ViewKeyCache`/`ViewSpecializationTicks` are not standalone caches: generic `Material2d` queueing consumes their keys/ticks to decide per-view specialization. They must be implemented together with S10 rather than added as unused resources. Bevy's `extract_mesh2d` rebuilds all visible instances each frame; material specialization separately observes `Changed<Mesh2d>` and `AssetChanged<Mesh2d>`. |
| S10 | Generic `Material2d`, `Material2dPlugin`, specialization caches, prepared material properties, `MeshMaterial2d<M>`, and `ColorMaterial` | generic `Material2d`/`Material2dPlugin<M>`, `MeshMaterial2d<M>` component, `ColorMaterial`; draw functions stored per material instead of parameterised (S10f) | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17 (audit complete; ready to implement).** Read pinned `bevy_sprite_render/src/mesh2d/material.rs` against the current Epix types. Bevy's surface to match: `Material2d: AsBindGroup + Asset + Clone` with defaults `vertex_shader()`/`fragment_shader()` (`ShaderRef::Default`), `depth_bias(&self) -> f32` = 0, `alpha_mode(&self) -> AlphaMode2d` = `Opaque`, and `specialize(&mut RenderPipelineDescriptor, &MeshVertexBufferLayoutRef, Material2dKey<Self>)` (material.rs:131-167); `MeshMaterial2d<M>(pub Handle<M>)` as a component that is `Default` (invalid handle), `PartialEq`/`Eq` by handle, `From<MeshMaterial2d<M>> for AssetId<M>` and `AsAssetId` (199-233); `Material2dPlugin<M>` registering `Assets<M>`, `EntitiesNeedingSpecialization<M>`, `RenderAssetPlugin<PreparedMaterial2d<M>>`, the PostUpdate `check_entities_needing_specialization<M>` after `AssetEventSystems`, and in the render world `EntitySpecializationTickPair<M>`, `SpecializedMaterial2dPipelineCache<M>`, `add_render_command` for all three Core2D phases with `DrawMaterial2d<M>`, `RenderMaterial2dInstances<M>`, `SpecializedMeshPipelines<Material2dPipeline<M>>`, `init_material_2d_pipeline<M>.after(init_mesh_2d_pipeline)`, extraction (`extract_entities_needs_specialization<M>.after(extract_cameras)`, `extract_mesh_materials_2d<M>`) and the PrepareMeshes/QueueMeshes systems (271-319); `RenderMaterial2dInstances<M>` is a `MainEntity -> AssetId<M>` map (322), `Material2dPipeline<M>` holds the Mesh2d pipeline, the material layout and both shader handles (382-388), and `Material2dKey<M>` is `{ Mesh2dPipelineKey, M::Data }` with equality over both (390-404). Already satisfied in Epix: `AsBindGroup` with the specialization-plus-concept pattern, and `AlphaMode2d` as a Bevy-shaped variant (`mesh2d.hpp:29-38`). **New finding that changes the plan:** a non-template class and a class template cannot share a name in C++, and the current fixed `MeshMaterial2d{.color=..., .alpha_mode=...}` has **65 construction sites** in the tree (`pixel_body_sand`, `falling_sand`, `liquid_eulerian`, `batching`, `alpha_mask`, and the mesh/text examples). The `MeshMaterial2d<M>` swap, `ColorMaterial`, and those 65 sites must therefore land as one atomic step, because the moment the component becomes a template every struct-literal construction stops compiling - and each migrated site needs an `Assets<ColorMaterial>` source, so the examples' setup systems change too. The earlier decomposition remains the plan (S10a trait surface and component, S10b `ColorMaterial` and its `AsBindGroup`, S10c `Material2dPlugin`/`Material2dPipeline`/`Material2dKey`/`init_material_2d_pipeline`/`SetMaterial2dBindGroup`, S10d the specialization caches, `RenderMaterial2dInstances`, `extract_mesh_materials_2d`, `extract_entities_needs_specialization`, S10e rewiring `mesh2d.cpp` extraction and the pipeline), with the correction that S10a's component half and S10b's site migration are one atomic commit rather than independent steps. The current fixed materials are not Bevy's generic material asset pipeline. **Decomposition established (2026-09-11):** `AsBindGroup` and `AsBindGroupShaderType` already exist in `epix_render` with the specialization-plus-concept pattern (`AsBindGroup<C>`, `AsBindGroupImpl`, `PreparedBindGroup`, `UnpreparedBindGroup`, the three `AsBindGroupError` alternatives), so the trait dependency `Material2d: AsBindGroup` is **satisfied** —it is not the blocker. The work splits into: **S10a** `Material2d<M>` specialization surface (`vertex_shader`/`fragment_shader` returning `shader::ShaderRef`, `depth_bias`, `alpha_mode`, `specialize`) plus the `Material2dImpl` concept and the generic `MeshMaterial2d<M>` component; **S10b** `ColorMaterial` (Bevy `color_material.rs`, 5 KB) and its `AsBindGroup<ColorMaterial>` specialization, replacing the fixed `MeshMaterial2d`/`MeshTextureMaterial2d`; **S10c** `Material2dPlugin<M>` with `Material2dPipeline<M>`, `Material2dKey<M>`, `init_material_2d_pipeline`, and `SetMaterial2dBindGroup`; **S10d** the derivative caches `SpecializedMaterial2dPipelineCache<M>` / `SpecializedMaterial2dViewPipelineCache<M>`, `RenderMaterial2dInstances<M>`, `extract_mesh_materials_2d`, and `extract_entities_needs_specialization`; **S10e** rewiring `sprite_render`'s extraction (currently `mesh2d.cpp:581-610`, fixed-struct based) and the mesh pipeline to consume `MeshMaterial2d<M>`. **Blast radius for S10a alone is 45+ call sites**: every mesh/sprite/text/fallingsand/pixelbody example and system constructs `MeshMaterial2d{.color=..., .alpha_mode=...}` as a struct literal, and `mesh2d.cpp`'s `extract_meshes_2d` reads `.color`, `.alpha_mode`, and `.image` directly. S10d/S10e are what make S9 possible, so the sequence must run a→b→c→d→e rather than landing the trait alone. Not started: landing S10a in isolation would leave two competing material representations in the tree. **Ordering correction (verified 2026-09-11): the real blocker is S10c's uniform-buffer binding infrastructure, not the trait.** Bevy's `ColorMaterial` binds a `ColorMaterialUniform` at `@group(2) @binding(0)` (Bevy `color_material.rs:38`); Epix's `OwnedBindingResource` represents that case as `OwnedData`, whose own doc comment in `as_bind_group.hpp:77-79` states it is "not directly a WebGPU binding; a higher-level allocator must first materialize it in a buffer", and `BindingResources::entries` deliberately returns `CreateBindGroupDirectly` for it (`:144-146`). So S10b cannot be implemented before the material uniform allocator exists, and the a→b→c order must instead be **a→c→b→d→e**. Recording this so the next attempt starts at S10c rather than discovering the same wall in S10b. **Correction (2026-09-11):** the earlier claim that S10c's uniform infrastructure is entirely missing is wrong and is superseded. `epix_engine/render/render/include/epix/render/render_resource.hpp` already provides `UniformBuffer`, `DynamicUniformBuffer` (with `push`/`write_buffer`/`get_writer` and a `DynamicUniformBufferWriter`), `DynamicUniformBuffer<T>::new_with_alignment`, `BatchedUniformBuffer`, `GpuArrayBuffer` and `InstanceInputUniformBuffer`, so the material-uniform allocator S10b/S10c need only the `AsBindGroup` `OwnedData` materialization (the "higher-level allocator" `as_bind_group.hpp` documents) plus `Material2dPlugin`. That narrowing does not change the S10a-e decomposition or its a-c-b-d-e order. |
| S10f | `DrawMaterial2d<M>` — a draw command parameterised by the material type, so one `add_render_command` registration serves every material | `Material2dDrawFunctions<M>` holding the three `DrawFunctionId`s returned by `app_add_render_commands`, selected at prepare time from the material's `AlphaMode2d` | FIXED | **Landed 2026-10 (commit `9f37ee6c`); the "sanctioned divergence" note that follows is superseded and kept only as history.** The render-command API now matches Bevy's trait shape: `RenderCommand<C, P>` is a trait struct carrying `Param`/`ViewQuery`/`ItemQuery` and the render function, commands are phase-independent tag structs with one constrained partial specialisation each, and registration and lookup are type-based (`app_add_render_command<C>`, `DrawFunctions<P>::read()->id<C>()`), so `prepare` resolves the material draw function per phase exactly as Bevy does (`material.rs:1000-1005`) and `Material2dDrawFunctions<M>` is gone. The same work corrected a mismatch in the concept itself: Bevy bounds both render-command queries with `ReadOnlyQueryData` and the param with `ReadOnlySystemParam` (`draw.rs:196,202,212`), whereas Epix's concept accepted mutable view and item queries. | Bevy registers `DrawMaterial2d<M>` once per material type and the compiler resolves `M` through the render command's template parameter. C++ cannot pass a *dependent* template-template argument through Epix's `app_add_render_commands<P, R...>` (whose `R` must itself be a template over the phase), so `Material2dPlugin<M>::ready` stores the three returned `DrawFunctionId`s into a `Material2dDrawFunctions<M>` resource and `prepare_asset` picks among them by alpha mode — which is the same information Bevy encodes in its type, resolved earlier. Behaviour is equivalent: one pipeline and one draw function per `(material type, alpha mode)`. Recorded so it is not mistaken for an unexplained workaround; it cannot be retired by a future standard, only by Epix gaining dependent template-template parameters. |
| S11 | `Wireframe2dPlugin`, phase/item/keys, material/extraction/specialization, config and marker components | `Wireframe2dPlugin` in `sprite_render` with the same binned phase item, batch-set/bin keys, `PolygonMode::Line` pipeline, push-constant colour, node placement between `EndMainPass` and `PostProcessing`, and the `Wireframe2d`/`NoWireframe2d`/`Wireframe2dColor`/`Wireframe2dConfig`/`Mesh2dWireframe` surface | ✅ | **Closed 2026-10.** Ported item by item from `bevy_sprite_render/src/mesh2d/wireframe2d.rs`; the plugin is opt-in exactly as Bevy's is, the colour uses the real 16-byte fragment push constant, and no wireframe mesh is generated (the original mesh is drawn with polygon-mode lines). Verified by the new `sprite_render/examples/header/wireframe2d.cpp` capture, which shows the global, per-entity-colour and opt-out paths in one frame, and by six new tests in `sprite_render/tests/header/wireframe2d_tests.cpp` (phase-item concepts and accessors, batch-set/bin key ordering and hashing, config defaults, embedded-shader registration, material preparation and `AsAssetId`). |
| S12 | Sprite render extraction, events, batching, image/view bind groups, phase queueing, and draw commands | resource-backed Bevy-shaped sprite renderer; detailed by `documentation/sprite-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` S12a-S12i/S12x | ✅ | S12a-S12i now match the pinned extraction, event/cache, queue, per-view batch, buffer, draw-command, scheduling, and IEEE scaling contracts with direct regressions. S12x remains the documented intentional `CustomRendered` opt-out extension. |
| S13 | Texture-atlas-aware sprite sizing, UV selection, extraction, and pixel-space conversion | image-owned atlas types consumed by CPU and render sprite paths | ✅| Atlas region sizing, extraction UV selection, optional sprite rect offset, and pixel-space conversion are implemented and covered by unit and stable three-frame visual checks. |
| S14 | `TilemapChunk`, tile data/storage, mesh cache, material, packing/image creation, plugin systems, and rendering | `TilemapChunk`/`TileData`/`TilemapChunkTileData`/`TilemapChunkMeshCache`/`PackedTileData`/`TilemapChunkMaterial` and both plugins in `sprite_render/{include/epix/sprite_render/tilemap_chunk.hpp,src/tilemap_chunk.cpp,src/tilemap_chunk_material.cpp}` | ✅ | **Closed 2026-10.** Ported item by item from `bevy_sprite_render/src/tilemap_chunk/{mod.rs,tilemap_chunk_material.rs,tilemap_chunk_material.wgsl}`, with the `on_insert` hook building the chunk mesh, the `Rgba16Uint` per-tile data image and the material, `calculate_tile_transform` on `TilemapChunk`, `tile_data_from_tile_pos`, `update_tilemap_chunk_indices` rewriting the packed image in place, and the 8-byte `PackedTileData` ABI the Slang fragment shader decodes (`Texture2DArray<float4>` tileset + `Texture2D<uint4>` tile data + filtering sampler). Verified by 11 tests in `sprite_render/tests/header/tilemap_chunk_tests.cpp` and the `sprite_render/examples/{header,module}/tilemap_chunk.cpp` capture; the recorded divergences (immutable/reflect attributes, direct hook insert instead of `world.commands()`, warn instead of panic, linear `glm::vec4` tint packed through the sRGB transfer, `image::DEFAULT_IMAGE_HANDLE` defaults, and Bevy's never-populated mesh cache reproduced verbatim) are listed in `documentation/sprite-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` S14. |
| S15 | `extract_text2d_sprite` emits glyph sprites into the shared sprite render pipeline | `epix::text::extract_text2d_sprite` emits `ExtractedSprite{kind: Slices}` + `ExtractedSlice` into the sprite renderer's stores, which `queue_sprites_2d` and `SpritePipeline` then draw | ✅ | **Closed 2026-10 (commit `b3fc90c7`).** The separate text mesh renderer (bespoke `text-` pipeline, its Slang shaders, a texture-array atlas, a `TextMesh` per entity and a `TextImage` component) is deleted. `extract_text2d_sprite` mirrors `bevy_sprite_render/src/text2d/mod.rs:25-273`: one `ExtractedSlice{offset = (position.x, -position.y), rect, size}` per glyph, one `ExtractedSprite{kind: Slices{begin,end}, image_handle_id = glyph.atlas_info.texture}` per run of glyphs sharing an atlas texture, `TemporaryRenderEntity` per sprite, extraction gated on `ViewVisibility` and ordered `.after(SpriteSystems::ExtractSprites)` as Bevy's `.after(SpriteSystems::ExtractSprites)` registration is (`lib.rs:104-105`). Text now links `epix_sprite`/`epix_sprite_render`, and the geometry helper `detail::extract_text2d_layout` is unit-tested. The single intentional structural difference is that the system lives in the text module because Epix's dependency direction is text to sprite-render, not the reverse. |
| S16 | Bevy sprite/mesh2d shader libraries, pipeline layouts, tonemapping/debanding keys, and per-view bind-group contracts | sprite uses an equivalent embedded Slang shader, Bevy-shaped layouts, keys, and bind groups; detailed by `documentation/sprite-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` S16a-S16e | ✅ | Sprite S16a-S16e are aligned, including the exact 80-byte vertex ABI and LDR tonemapping. Slang remains the accepted representation adaptation. Mesh2d's separate shader/pipeline work remains tracked under S9/S10. |

## Image

The image loader may use Epix's selected C++ decoding libraries and need not
copy Bevy's Rust `image`/Basis/KTX2/DDS implementation. That exception covers
the decoder backend and the set of formats the backend can actually decode;
it does not waive Bevy-facing asset, settings, image-data, atlas, or render
preparation semantics.

**Re-audited 2026-09-17.** Every row below was re-checked against the current
sources after the image module's own batch landed. The `I1`–`I5`, `I9` and
`I13` ❌ marks recorded here were stale: they described a `Image` shape that no
longer exists. The per-item `G`-rows in
`documentation/image-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` remain the authoritative
record; this section states the module-level status and the divergences that
stay open.

| ID | Bevy entity / behavior | Epix counterpart | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| I1 | `Image` owns optional bytes, `TextureDataOrder`, full texture descriptor, optional view descriptor, `RenderAssetUsages`, sampler, and copy-on-resize | `texture_descriptor` as the source of truth, `data_order`, `texture_view_descriptor`, `asset_usage`/`usage()`, `m_sampler`, `copy_on_resize`, plus a byte vector with a presence flag | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: this row's ❌ was stale; the work landed with the image module's own batch.** `image.hpp:968` owns `wgpu::TextureDescriptor texture_descriptor`, and `width()`/`height()`/`depth_or_layers()`/`depth()`/`layers()`/`type()`/`texture_format()` all read from it (`:977-995`), so the four former intrinsic fields are gone (image tracker G1). `data_order` (`:971`), `texture_view_descriptor` (`:974`), `copy_on_resize` (`:802`, accessor `:1005`) and `asset_usage` as `assets::RenderAssetUsages` (`:800`, G2) all exist. Direct wgpu descriptor types are the accepted translation; the sampler types stay Bevy-owned (G10). The one remaining representational divergence is the byte payload: `std::vector<std::byte>` plus `bool m_has_data` rather than `std::optional<std::vector<std::byte>>` (G3, ⚠️), whose observable present/absent contract was independently verified under P5. |
| I2 | `Image` constructors and size/resize/reinterpret surface: `new`, `new_uninit`, `new_fill`, target texture, `size`, `size_f32`, aspect ratio, resize-in-place, reinterpret size/stacked arrays, compression query | `Image::new`/`new_uninit`, `size`/`size_f32`/`aspect_ratio`, `resize_size`/`resize_in_place`, `reinterpret_size`/`reinterpret_stacked_2d_as_array`, `is_compressed()`, typed `TextureReinterpretationError` | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: implemented** (image tracker G11–G16, G32–G34). The retained Epix-only extensions are recorded rather than silently kept: `Image::resize(w, h)` (resampling) and `Image::blur(radius)` have no Bevy counterpart (G15, ⚠️) and are used by existing callers, not by parity-critical paths. |
| I3 | Pixel offset/byte/color access, clear, typed `TextureAccessError`, conversion errors, and exact dimensional/layer behavior | `pixel_data_offset`, tagged `TextureAccessError`, `pixel_bytes`/`pixel_bytes_mut`, `clear`, `get_color_at*`/`set_color_at*`, `to_string(TextureAccessError)` | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: implemented** (image tracker G12, G17–G20, G26 and G33). Bevy's `OutOfBounds`/`UnsupportedTextureFormat`/`WrongDimension` alternatives, the compressed-format rejection, and the exact dimensional/layer arithmetic are covered by the image module's own tests. |
| I4 | `ImageSampler` tagged union: `Default` or descriptor payload, linear/nearest helpers, mutable descriptor initialization | payload-carrying `ImageSampler` (`DefaultAlternative`/`DescriptorAlternative`) with `linear()`, `nearest()` and `get_or_init_descriptor`; no separate descriptor member | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: implemented** (image tracker G8). |
| I5 | Full sampler descriptor and conversion surface, including anisotropic helper and `OpaqueWhite`/`Zero` border variants | `ImageSamplerDescriptor` with all six helpers including `as_wgpu()` and `set_anisotropic_filter()`, and the border-colour variants | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: implemented** (image tracker G9, with G10 confirming that the sampler types must stay Bevy-owned rather than aliasing wgpu's). |
| I6 | `TextureAtlasPlugin`, `TextureAtlasLayout`, `TextureAtlas`, and `TextureAtlasSources` with grid/layout/look-up helpers | image-owned matching atlas facilities | ✅ | Grid ordering/extents, layout mutation, selected rectangles, source index/rect/UV/handle lookup, plugin installation, and actual sprite rendering are tested. |
| I7 | `TextureAtlasBuilder`, typed packing errors, format conversion, padding, atlas-source mapping, and output image | `TextureAtlasBuilder` + `TextureAtlasBuilderError`, on a port of the `rectangle-pack` 0.4 crate | ✅ | Implemented and verified 2026-09-12 (image tracker G24 and G24c). The packer Bevy packs through is ported rather than replaced, so the packing contract is Bevy's own: same group ordering, same split choice, same bin doubling. Every placement, error and copied atlas byte is asserted against the real `bevy_image 0.18.0` builder through `tmp/bevy-image-fixture`; see `documentation/image-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` G24 for the scenario list and the two recorded adaptations (`Image::new`/`pixel_size`/`convert(TextureFormat)` are now ported as G32-G34; the `image`-crate conversion math stays deferred under G25d). |
| I8 | `DynamicTextureAtlasBuilder`, incremental allocator, padding, copy placement, and typed errors | `DynamicTextureAtlasBuilder` + `DynamicTextureAtlasBuilderError`, on a port of the `guillotiere` 0.6.0 allocator | ✅ | Implemented and verified 2026-09-12 (image tracker G24a/G24b). The `guillotiere` allocator is transcribed so allocation order is the crate's, and the builder is asserted against the real crate's placements and pixel copies; see `documentation/image-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` G24a/G24b. |
| I9 | `ImagePlugin` registers images/fallbacks and preregisters the loader; render-side image preparation remains composed by the render layer | `ImagePlugin` (image.cpp:1426) registers the image store, the loader and the fallback assets; `render/src/image.cpp:12` installs `RenderAssetPlugin<image::Image>` | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: implemented.** Ownership matches Bevy: the CPU-facing `ImagePlugin` owns the assets/loader/fallbacks (asserted by `ImagePlugin.InstallsBevyFallbackImageAssets` and `ImagePlugin.ExposesBevyDefaultSamplerChoices` in `image_plugin_tests.cpp`), while the render-world `RenderAssetPlugin<Image>` is composed by the render layer exactly as Bevy's `RenderPlugin` installs `ImageRenderPlugin`. The explicit `AssetPlugin` prerequisite is preserved. |
| I10 | `ImageLoaderSettings`: format selection/guessing, optional texture format, sRGB, sampler, asset usage, and array layout; typed loader errors | full `ImageLoaderSettings` consumed by `ImageLoader::load`; typed `ImageLoaderError` | ✅ | Implemented and verified 2026-09-11 (G22). Every setting is applied in Bevy's order: `format` selects `FromExtension`/`Format`/`Guess`, `from_buffer` applies `is_srgb`, `sampler` and `asset_usage`, `texture_format` then overrides the descriptor, and `array_layout` reinterprets the result with Bevy's `RowCount`/`RowHeight` arithmetic and its typed `ImageLoaderError::ArrayLayout` failure. Decoder implementation and the set of file formats Epix's `stb_image` can read intentionally differ (see I11). Verified by the whole-tree rebuild, 75/75 CTest executables, and the new `loader_settings` example whose two tiles show the sRGB and linear samplings of one palette; see `documentation/image-bevy-0.18-mismatch-tracker.md` G22 for the full evidence. |
| I11 | Public format/MIME/extension mapping, texture errors, compressed-format capability selection, and optional EXR/HDR/KTX2/DDS/Basis paths | `ImageFormat` with Bevy's extension/MIME tables, `is_decodable`, `to_string`; typed unsupported-format results | ✅ | Implemented and verified 2026-09-11 (G22/G23). Loading-library/capability divergence accepted and enumerated: `hdr` is decoded by the single `ImageLoader` rather than a separate `HdrTextureLoader`, `exr` is named and rejected with `UnsupportedTextureFormat("OpenExr")` instead of `ExrTextureLoader`, and `pic`/`psd` are Epix-only alternatives the decoder reads. `SUPPORTED_FORMATS` and the advertised extension list are derived the way Bevy derives them, so they cannot drift apart. |
| I12 | `SerializedImage` feature for short-lived cross-process transmission | none | ?? | Deferred, and recorded as such in the image tracker (G25b): the `serialize`-gated transfer type has no Epix consumer yet, and the mesh module's own lossy transfer type (M21) covers the one in-engine use. |
| I13 | Render extraction/preparation respects descriptors, view descriptors, data order, mip/layer layout, previous-image copy-on-resize, and retry errors | `RenderAsset<Image>` / `GpuImage` preparation in `render/src/image.cpp` | ✅ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: implemented.** Preparation builds the GPU image from the asset's own descriptor (`:79-116`), lets `texture_view_descriptor` override the derived view (`:94`, image tracker G29), copies the previous GPU image when the upload has no data and `copy_on_resize` is set (`:126`), passes `data_order` to the layered upload and asserts the single-mip invariant it currently requires (`:148-158`), and reports usage from the asset (`:197`). The render-only payload transfer this depends on is P5. |

## Core Pipeline Plugin and Shared Facilities

| ID | Bevy entity / behavior | Epix counterpart | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | `CorePipelinePlugin`: installs 2D, 3D, blit, tonemapping, upscaling, OIT, mip generation, deferred-copy plugins | `CoreGraphPlugin` | ⚠️ | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: the note below was stale.** `CoreGraphPlugin` (`core_graph/src/plugin.cpp:53-54`) installs `Core2dPlugin`, `Core3dPlugin`, `BlitPlugin`, `UpscalingPlugin`, `TonemappingPlugin` and `CopyDeferredLightingIdPlugin`, and it initializes the shared fullscreen shader. Core3D therefore landed with C13–C15, and the deferred-copy plugin with C15-D4; the earlier claim that "Core3D, deferred-copy, blit, upscaling and tonemapping remain missing" described the pre-C13 state. What is genuinely absent from Bevy's `CorePipelinePlugin` list is the OIT subsystem (C18), the experimental depth mip-generation plugin (C19) and the skybox plugin (C20), each retained in its own row; the Core2D tonemapping node is a real node, not a placeholder. |
| C2 | `FullscreenShader`: shared embedded fullscreen-triangle vertex shader and `to_vertex_state()` | `core_graph::FullscreenShader`, initialized by `CoreGraphPlugin` | ✅| CoreGraphPlugin directly mirrors Bevy CorePipelinePlugin ownership: embeds the shared shader, installs core plugins, then initializes the render-world resource. Core2D only consumes it. Slang remains the accepted shader-language divergence. |
| C3 | `FullscreenMaterial<T>` and `FullscreenMaterialPlugin<T>`: extracted uniform component, two HDR/non-HDR pipelines, a typed view node, and explicit graph edges | `core_graph::FullscreenMaterial`, `FullscreenMaterialPlugin<T>`, `FullscreenMaterialNode<T>`, `FullscreenMaterialPipeline` | ✅ | The material contract returns `ShaderRef`, render startup resolves handle/path references through the render-world `AssetServer`, the pass uses Bevy's default load operation, and the LDR pipeline uses the Bevy-default sRGB target format. **Closed 2026-09-11:** `extract_on_add` now matches Bevy's routing exactly (`fullscreen_material.rs:109-117`): `Core3d` when the entity is a `Camera3d`, otherwise `Core2d` when it is a `Camera2d`, otherwise warn and skip, so a 3D fullscreen material is possible now that C13 built Core3d. |
| C4 | General `BlitPlugin`, `BlitPipeline`, and `BlitPipelineKey` | `core_graph::BlitPlugin`, `BlitPipeline`, and `BlitPipelineKey` | ✅| Reusable core-graph facility now owns an embedded fragment shader, render-startup resource initialization, specialized-pipeline cache, direct-WGPU bind-group creation, and format/blend/sample specialization. Core2D's private output blit remains separately audited under C17. |
| C4a | `SpecializedRenderPipeline` / `SpecializedComputePipeline` specialize from `&self` | `render::SpecializedRenderPipeline` / `render::SpecializedComputePipeline` concepts | ✅| Pipeline instances own `Key` and `specialize(key)`, and cache methods receive the specialization instance exactly as in Bevy. |
| C4b | `GetFullBatchData::BufferInputData` bound: `Pod + Default + Sync + Send` (`batching/mod.rs:109`) | `batching::GetFullBatchDataImpl` requires `RawBufferElement` + `default_initializable` | ✅ | **Re-aligned 2026-10 (found via M-series mesh2d work).** The concept previously required `render_resource::ShaderWritable<BufferInputData>` — a *shader-description* requirement (it needs a `ShaderTypeInfo` marker). Bevy requires only a *storage* requirement, and `Mesh2dPipeline` legitimately uses the unit type (`type BufferInputData = ()`, `mesh2d/mesh.rs:354`) because its GPU preprocessing path is unimplemented, so nothing is ever uploaded. `std::monostate` has no `ShaderTypeInfo`, so the stricter bound rejected a case Bevy permits; MSVC surfaced it only as `could not deduce template argument for 'Ts &&' from 'overloaded-function'` at every registration site, with no deeper note. Now bounded by `render_resource::PodElement` plus `default_initializable`. `PodElement` approximates `bytemuck::Pod` with what C++ can actually decide — bytewise-copyable **and** standard-layout — with `PodElementInfo` as an *escape hatch* for a type that is `Pod` but that the automatic check cannot accept (the unverifiable part being the absence of padding, which needs reflection). No specialization is required for `int`, `float`, `std::monostate`, or any other type C++, so **the escape hatch is currently unused by the whole tree**, which is the healthy state and means it can be deleted outright once reflection lands. **Recorded divergences:** C++ has no `Send + Sync`, so those two bounds are omitted; and `PodElement` remains an approximation of `Pod` on the validity axis (`Zeroable`, every bit pattern valid), not a proof of it — same divergence family as the C++26 static-reflection TODO at `render_resource.hpp:588`, since Bevy derives these traits while Epix approximates them. |

## Core 2D Pipeline

| ID | Bevy entity / behavior | Epix counterpart | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C5 | `Core2dPlugin` required components, phase extraction, depth preparation, graph registration | `Core2dPlugin` | ✅| The plugin now adds Bevy's `DebandDither::Disabled`, `CameraRenderGraph{Core2d}`, and `Tonemapping::None` requirements to `Camera2d`, and registers `ExtractComponentPlugin<Camera2d>`. Phase/depth/graph details remain separately tracked under C6–C12 and C17; the actual tonemapping pipeline is C16. |
| C6 | `core_2d::graph::Core2d` and all graph node labels | `Core2dGraph`, `Core2dNodes` | ✅| `Core2dNodes` now exposes Bevy's complete public label set. Core2D installs exactly Bevy's eight default nodes and chain: main passes, post-processing endpoints, tonemapping integration point, and upscaling. MSAA writeback, wireframe, bloom, general post-processing, FXAA, SMAA, and CAS remain labels for their owning optional plugins. The actual tonemapping node implementation is C16. |
| C7 | Binned `Opaque2d`, `AlphaMask2d`, their bin keys, and batch-set key | Binned `Opaque2D` / `AlphaMask2D`, `BatchSetKey2D`, and mesh producer | ✅| Core2D now uses two Bevy-shaped binned phase types. Mesh extraction writes `RenderMesh2dInstances`, keyed by `MainEntity` exactly as Bevy, so placeholder binned render entities never become an implicit data dependency. The C++ `MeshAlphaMode2d` tagged union covers `Opaque`, `Mask(cutoff)`, and `Blend`; mask pipelines discard below the cutoff and retain depth writes. |
| C8 | Sorted `Transparent2d` phase item | `Transparent2D` | ✅ | Entity, main entity, cached pipeline, draw function, range, extra index, and the `indexed` discriminator correspond. Mesh queueing reads `GPUMesh::is_indexed`; sprite and text producers explicitly report non-indexed geometry. **Reopened 2026-09-16 (C8-D1/C8-D2):** this row previously recorded "reverse-depth sorting" as if it matched Bevy, but `Transparent2D::sort_key()` negated the depth and the phase used an unstable sort. Both are now fixed and asserted by `core_pipeline_tests.cpp`. **Closed 2026-09-28:** re-verified against Bevy - `Transparent2D::sort_key()` returns the un-negated view-space `z`, and `sort()` is a stable ascending sort (`std::stable_sort`), matching `Transparent2d::sort`'s `radsort::sort_by_key` (`core_2d/mod.rs:408-420`) in both ordering and stability; only the algorithm differs (comparison sort vs radix sort), which is unobservable, and the smallest key draws first, which is back-to-front. |
| C8-D1 | `Transparent2d::sort_key()` returns the raw `FloatOrd(translation.z)`; `SortedPhaseItem::sort` draws the smallest key first, so ascending `z` is back-to-front for the reverse-Z Core2D camera | `Transparent2D::sort_key()` returned `-depth` while every producer (sprite, mesh2d, text) stored `translation.z` in `depth`, so the phase was sorted front-to-back and nearer items were painted first | ✅ | Fixed 2026-09-16: `sort_key()` returns `depth` unchanged. The visible proof is the `texture_slice` example, whose deliberate 170x130 backing plate sits at `z=-0.1` behind a 160x120 sprite at `z=0`: before the fix only the plate was drawn (the sprite was painted over), after it the sprite is drawn over the plate and the plate shows only as the intended frame. `Transparent2D.SortsBackToFrontByRawDepthAndKeepsEqualDepthStable` asserts the key and the resulting order. |
| C8-D2 | `Transparent2d::sort` uses a stable sort (`radsort::sort_by_key`), so equal-depth items keep their queue order | The generic `SortedRenderPhase::sort` fell back to `std::ranges::sort` (unstable) because `Transparent2D` provided no `sort` override | ✅ | Fixed 2026-09-16: `Transparent2D` now provides the static `sort` override with `std::stable_sort` by `sort_key()`, matching Bevy's stated stability contract. Bevy's radix sort orders `-0.0` before `+0.0` and orders NaNs by bit pattern, while a comparison sort treats those as equivalent and keeps their input order; this is recorded as an adaptation because the two agree on every non-NaN, non-signed-zero key. Asserted by the same test's equal-depth case. |
| C9 | `extract_core_2d_camera_phases` maintains opaque, alpha-mask, transparent phase resources | `extract_core_2d_camera_phases` | ✅| Re-audited and corrected: public helper with Bevy's name, active Camera2d query independent of CameraRenderGraph, Local live-view set reuse, transparent clearing, both binned phases prepared with None, and dead-view retention. Core-graph-owned tests cover custom/missing graphs and inactive/despawned cameras. |
| C10 | `prepare_core_2d_depth_textures` cached depth textures per target and MSAA samples | `prepare_core_2d_depth_textures` (Core2D) | ✅| Re-audited and corrected: explicit With<Camera2d> on the synced view entity, both phase-membership checks, optional-target cache key (including None with a physical size), and public CORE_2D_DEPTH_FORMAT. GPU regression covers rejected non-2D/missing-phase/missing-size views, shared targets, MSAA, depth format/usage, and first-use reverse-Z clearing over two frames. Scheduled in PrepareResources; generic ViewPlugin does not allocate depth. |

| C11 | `MainOpaquePass2dNode` with independent opaque + alpha-mask pass work | `MainOpaquePass2DNode` | ✅| The dedicated typed view node resolves both binned phases and records them, in order, into one deferred generated command buffer with Bevy-matching color/depth attachments and camera viewport. |
| C12 | `MainTransparentPass2dNode` clears/render transparent pass independently | `MainTransparentPass2DNode` | ✅| A separate typed view node records the retained sorted transparent phase into its own deferred generated command buffer. WebGL-only viewport reset remains irrelevant to the forced Vulkan path. |

## Core 3D and Pipeline Stages

| ID | Bevy entity / behavior | Epix counterpart | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C13 | `Core3dPlugin` base pipeline | `epix::core_graph::core_3d` (`Core3d`, `Core3dNodes`, `Core3dPlugin`) | ✅ | Base pipeline implemented: C13a-C13c are done and the 3D graph, phase, depth and transmission surface now mirrors Bevy 0.18 `core_3d/mod.rs`. C13d stays open only for the on-screen proof of the distinct main-3D phase ordering, which needs a 3D draw pipeline that does not exist yet (no `Mesh3d` render/material pipeline). Prepass and deferred are now real nodes owned by C14/C15, and C15-D4 closed the last `EmptyNode`, so every label in Bevy's default Core3D chain is a real node. Adapted/divergent details are listed under "Core 3D Intentional Divergences". |
| C13a | `core_3d::graph::{Core3d, Node3d}`, four main 3D phase types/keys, plugin resources, extraction, and sorting | `epix::core_graph::core_3d::Core3d`/`Core3dNodes`, `Opaque3d`, `AlphaMask3d`, `Transmissive3d`, `Transparent3d`, `extract_core_3d_camera_phases` | ✅| All 30 Bevy labels are declared in Bevy's order (asserted by ordinal in tests). `Opaque3dBatchSetKey`/`Opaque3dBinKey` and `OpaqueNoLightmap3dBatchSetKey`/`BinKey` mirror Bevy's fields including vertex, index and lightmap slab identity (P6 supplies `mesh::SlabId`); `AlphaMask3d` uses the lightmap-free pair exactly as Bevy does, and that pair is declared once here so C14 reuses it instead of duplicating it. `Camera3d` gains `DebandDither::Enabled`, `CameraRenderGraph{Core3d}` and the default `Tonemapping`; `ExtractComponent<Camera3d>` sits beside `Camera2d`'s in `render/view.hpp`. `extract_core_3d_camera_phases` selects the preprocessing mode from `GpuPreprocessingSupport` and `NoIndirectDrawing`, prepares both binned phases for the new frame, clears both sorted phases, and retains only live views. |
| C13a-D1 | `Transmissive3d::sort` and `Transparent3d::sort` use a stable radix sort (`radsort::sort_by_key`) over ascending view-space distance | Both items returned the raw `distance` key, but provided no `sort` override, so they inherited the generic unstable `std::ranges::sort` | ✅ | Fixed 2026-09-16: both items now provide the static `sort` override using `std::stable_sort` by `sort_key()`. The key itself was already correct (it was never negated, unlike C8-D1). Asserted by `Transparent3d.SortsByAscendingDistanceAndKeepsEqualDistanceStable`. The radix-vs-comparison ordering note recorded on C8-D2 applies identically. |
| C13b | `prepare_core_3d_depth_textures`, `ViewTransmissionTexture`, and `prepare_core_3d_transmission_textures` | same names | ✅| Depth preparation unions each target's requested `Camera3dDepthTextureUsage` with the prepass `COPY_SRC` a `DepthPrepass` view requires (C14), caches one texture per (target, sample count) and honours `Clear(value)`/`Load` through `ViewDepthTexture::create(CachedTexture, optional<float>)`. Transmission preparation requires all four phases, non-empty transmissive work, a non-zero step count and a physical target size, caches one single-sample copy-destination/sampleable texture per target in the view's HDR/LDR format, and creates the linear sampler. |
| C13c | Main opaque, transmissive, and transparent pass nodes plus the default Core3D graph chain | `MainOpaquePass3dNode`, `MainTransmissivePass3dNode`, `MainTransparentPass3dNode`, `Core3dGraph` | ✅| The plugin installs Bevy's default chain (`EarlyPrepass` -> `EarlyDeferredPrepass` -> `LatePrepass` -> `LateDeferredPrepass` -> `CopyDeferredLightingId` -> `EndPrepasses` -> `StartMainPass` -> `MainOpaquePass` -> `MainTransmissivePass` -> `MainTransparentPass` -> `EndMainPass` -> `StartMainPassPostProcessing` -> `Tonemapping` -> `EndMainPassPostProcessing` -> `Upscaling`). Opaque then alpha-mask render in order into one pass against the shared depth target; transmissive splits its depth-sorted items into at most `Camera3d::screen_space_specular_transmission_steps` ranges with a main-texture copy before each range; transparent begins its pass only when it has work (Bevy's 3D behaviour, and deliberately unlike Core2D, whose pass must always run so it clears the background). Every node label is installed at Bevy's position, and since C15-D4 no label in the chain is an `EmptyNode`. |
| C13d | Core3D unit/GPU/visual verification | `core_graph/tests/header/core3d_tests.cpp`, `examples/header/core3d_clear.cpp`, `examples/header/core3d_prepass.cpp` | ⚠️ | Added and passing: label-order, graph node/edge, batch-set/bin key, binned-item, sorted-item (including `Transmissive3d::AUTOMATIC_BATCHING == false`), split-range, plugin/required-component/Camera3d-extraction, phase-lifetime, depth usage/load/cache, transmission allocation/skip, and —with C14/C15 —the prepass phase-item contracts, extraction (including the render-entity marker propagation and the deferred phases), texture allocation, the depth-prepass `COPY_SRC` union, and deferred MSAA suppression (16 tests). Two dedicated GLFW examples: `core3d_clear` renders a `Camera3d` through the Core3D graph; `core3d_prepass` drives the real prepass and deferred node bodies on the GPU with all four prepass components and displays the subsystem's own textures, in both the deferred and the `no-deferred` mode. **Re-verified 2026-09-29:** the row stays open for exactly one reason - proving the distinct main-3D phase ordering on screen requires queuing real 3D draw items, and the 3D mesh/material pipeline is still missing. The `pbr` module now exists, but it contains only `epix/pbr/mesh_pipeline_key.hpp` and its test: there is no `StandardMaterial`, no `MeshMaterial3d`, and no 3D mesh draw command anywhere in the tree. The rest of the row holds: 17 tests in `core3d_tests.cpp` and both GLFW examples (`core3d_clear`, `core3d_prepass`) exist and pass. |
| C14 | Prepass components, textures, nodes, and preparation | `core_3d` prepass surface: `DepthPrepass`/`NormalPrepass`/`MotionVectorPrepass`/`DeferredPrepass`, `ViewPrepassTextures`, `Opaque3dPrepass`/`AlphaMask3dPrepass`, `EarlyPrepassNode`/`LatePrepassNode`, `extract_camera_prepass_phase`, `prepare_prepass_textures` | ✅ | Implemented 2026-09-11 (C14), reapplied and re-verified in round 6. `NORMAL_PREPASS_FORMAT`/`MOTION_VECTOR_PREPASS_FORMAT` match Bevy's `prepass/mod.rs:52-53`; both prepass phase items are binned and share `OpaqueNoLightmap3dBatchSetKey`/`BinKey` exactly as `AlphaMask3d` does; the extraction prepares both binned phases only for an active `Camera3d` carrying a prepass component and drops them otherwise, using the same `GpuPreprocessingSupport` mode the main phases use; the preparation allocates only the requested depth/normal/motion-vector textures with Bevy's formats, usages, per-target caching and first-use black clear; and `EarlyPrepassNode` renders both phases with Bevy's attachment order, emptying the color-attachment list when no prepass color target exists, then copying the view depth into the prepass depth. **Deferred-only parts are C15.** The prepass textures are cached by `camera.target` alone, exactly as Bevy does (`.entry(camera.target.clone())`, `core_3d/mod.rs:1049`, `:1071`, `:1092`, `:1114`, `:1136`, `:1180`). **That is deliberately unlike the view depth texture**, which Bevy keys on `(camera.target, Msaa)` (`.entry((camera.target.clone(), msaa))`, `:837`) because a depth attachment must match the view's sample count while a prepass color attachment is created per target only. Both keys were re-read line by line in round 7; `DepthTextureKey{target, sample_count}` was left untouched because it already matches. **Recorded adaptation:** the two absent color slots in the prepass pass are default-constructed `RenderPassColorAttachment` values rather than Bevy's per-slot `None`, because wgpu's C descriptor takes values directly. **Two of my own deviations were removed on 2026-09-11 after review:** a `(target, sample count)` prepass cache key and `T::sub_graph()`-based routing in `extract_on_add`, both inventions rather than Bevy behaviour; see the audit log. |
| C14-D1 | Bevy's prepass nodes also draw the skybox when `SkyboxPrepassPipeline`/`SkyboxPrepassBindGroup` are present, and `prepare_core_3d_depth_textures` adds `COPY_SRC` when a depth prepass is attached | No skybox inputs on the prepass nodes; the `COPY_SRC` half *is* implemented | Epix has no skybox subsystem (C20), so the view query omits those two optional components instead of inventing placeholder state. The depth-usage half is honoured, which closes the C13-D5 divergence: a view with `DepthPrepass` unions `COPY_SRC` into its target's depth usage, asserted by a regression test. |
| C14-D2 | `LatePrepassNode` runs a second prepass after occlusion culling when indirect drawing is enabled, and `MainPassResolutionOverride`/`PreviousViewUniformOffset` are honoured | `LatePrepassNode` returns without drawing | Epix has no bin split into a previously-visible early set and a newly-visible late set (`PreviousViewUniformOffset` has no producer), so Bevy's own condition (`!occlusion_culling || no_indirect_drawing` → return) is the only reachable branch and the early node owns the whole prepass. The `OcclusionCulling` component and `NoIndirectDrawing` marker both exist; what is missing is the culling subsystem that fills the late bins. The node, label and graph edge are installed so the subsystem can fill them without touching Core3D. |
| C15 | Deferred phases, nodes, and lighting-id copy plugin | `Opaque3dDeferred`, `AlphaMask3dDeferred`, `ViewPrepassTextures::deferred`/`deferred_lighting_pass_id`, `EarlyDeferredGBufferPrepassNode`, `LateDeferredGBufferPrepassNode`, `prepare_prepass_textures`' deferred textures, merged `extract_camera_prepass_phase`, `check_msaa` | ✅ | Implemented 2026-09-11. `DEFERRED_PREPASS_FORMAT` (`eRGBA32Uint`) and `DEFERRED_LIGHTING_PASS_ID_FORMAT` (`eR8Uint`) match `deferred/mod.rs`; both deferred phase items are binned and reuse `OpaqueNoLightmap3dBatchSetKey`/`BinKey`, exactly as the prepass pair does; `prepare_prepass_textures` allocates the G-buffer and the lighting-pass id as single-sampled render-attachment/sampleable textures with a first-use black clear; and `EarlyDeferredGBufferPrepassNode` renders both phases with Bevy's attachment order (normals, motion vectors, G-buffer, lighting-pass id). Two of my own deviations were removed in round 7 and are recorded in the audit log: a WebGL-only `Load` load-op switch on `is_late`, which Bevy compiles out natively, and a separate `extract_camera_deferred_phase` system, which Bevy does not have. **Two further divergences the row previously claimed but the code did not do were found in round 8 by the new `core3d_prepass` visual example, and are now implemented:** emptying the color-attachment list when every slot is absent (Bevy `deferred/node.rs:202-205`) and copying the view depth texture into the prepass depth texture (Bevy `deferred/node.rs:260-267`). Without the copy a deferred view's `ViewPrepassTextures::depth` was never written at all. **Open half (C15-D1):** the *queuing* half is `MaterialPlugin`'s deferred support in the `pbr` module, so the bins are empty until that lands. `CopyDeferredLightingId` is a real node since C15-D4. |
| C15-D1 | `MaterialPlugin` queues a deferred view's meshes into `Opaque3dDeferred`/`AlphaMask3dDeferred` (`bevy_pbr::material`), and `DeferredPrepass` users get a deferred lighting pass | Core-graph side complete; no producer | Belongs to `pbr`'s material pipeline, which does not exist yet. The phases, draw functions, extraction, textures and nodes are all installed so the producer only has to queue into them. |
| C15-D2 | `LateDeferredGBufferPrepassNode` runs a second deferred pass when `OcclusionCulling` is present and indirect drawing is allowed, and the deferred G-buffer is double-buffered for TAA | `LateDeferredGBufferPrepassNode` returns without drawing | The `OcclusionCulling` component exists in Epix but nothing splits the bins into a previously-visible early set and a newly-visible late set (`PreviousViewUniformOffset` has no producer), so a late pass would draw the early bins twice. Bevy's own guard (`!occlusion_culling || no_indirect_drawing` → return) is the only reachable branch. The node, label and graph edge are installed so the subsystem can fill them without touching Core3D. |
| C15-D3 | `extract_camera_prepass_phase` also inserts/removes `DepthPrepassDoubleBuffer` and `DeferredPrepassDoubleBuffer` on the synced render entity (TAA) | Not implemented | Epix has no TAA double-buffering, and neither marker component exists. Every other marker insertion Bevy performs is implemented. |
| C15-D4 | `CopyDeferredLightingIdPlugin`: `copy_deferred_lighting_id.wgsl`, `CopyDeferredLightingIdPipeline`, `CopyDeferredLightingIdNode`, `DeferredLightingIdDepthTexture`, and `prepare_deferred_lighting_id_textures`, registered by `CorePipelinePlugin` (`lib.rs:44`) | `CopyDeferredLightingIdPlugin` in `core_graph/src/copy_deferred_lighting_id.cpp`, installed by `CoreGraphPlugin` beside Core2d/Core3d | ✅ | **Closed.** Every label in the Core3D chain is now a real node. `DeferredLightingIdDepthTexture` carries one single-sampled `eDepth16Unorm` texture per `DeferredPrepass` view with `RenderAttachment | CopySrc` usage (`copy_lighting_id.rs:160-193`); `CopyDeferredLightingIdNode` is a depth-only fullscreen pass with `depth_compare: Always` and `depth_write_enabled: true`, binding the view's `deferred_lighting_pass_id` as `texture_2d<u32>` in a one-entry fragment-visible layout and writing `id / 255` into `SV_Depth` (`copy_lighting_id.rs:57-96, 98-136`); Verified on the Epix side: its render preparation already honours that descriptor, using the `ImageType` mapping only as a fallback (`render/render/src/image.cpp:91-102`), and `FallbackImageCubemap` already exists - so **the image module needs no change for C20**. What C20 does need: the `Skybox` component with its `SkyboxUniforms`, the skybox pipeline and bind group built from the image's cube view, the prepass variant, and the integration point - Bevy 0.18 adds `SkyboxPlugin` from `Core3dPlugin` (`core_3d/mod.rs:150`) rather than registering a separate graph node, and Epix's `MainOpaquePass3dNode` ViewQuery carries a comment recording that Bevy's optional skybox input is deliberately omitted (`core3d.hpp:662`), which is the piece to add. Separately worth recording as an image-module divergence: Epix's own `ImageType` (`e1D`/`e2D`/`e2DArray`/`e3D`, `image.hpp:407-416`) has no counterpart in pinned 0.18 at all, where the dimension lives only in the texture and view descriptors; it survives in Epix as the fallback for `view_dimension_cast` and would need removing to match Bevy exactly. **C20 part 3 landed 2026-10:** `SkyboxPipelineId` and `SkyboxBindGroup` (the bind group plus the uniform's dynamic offset, Bevy's `(BindGroup, u32)`), the extraction of `Skybox`, and the three prepare systems - `prepare_skybox_uniforms` (deriving `brightness * exposure` and the inverse-rotation matrix and uploading them, since `ExtractComponent<C>::Out` is one component in Epix so the derivation and the upload share a system; the exposure comes from the extracted camera), `prepare_skybox_pipelines` (`RenderSystems::Prepare`) and `prepare_skybox_bind_groups` (`PrepareBindGroups`, binding the cubemap's cube view, its sampler, the dynamic view uniform and the dynamic skybox uniforms). `SkyboxUniforms` opts into the GPU layout contract the way the tree's other seventeen uniform types do (`ShaderTypeInfo<T> : RawShaderType<T>`; `ShaderTypeInfo` is Epix's counterpart of encase's `ShaderType`, which Bevy derives - the primary template is deliberately undefined so a user struct must state its layout, which is what turned the two layout mistakes below into pipeline errors instead of wrong pixels). Still to land: the visual example (the draw integration landed in part 4). **C20 part 4 landed 2026-10:** `MainOpaquePass3dNode` now draws the skybox - its `ViewQuery` gained the view's `ViewUniformOffset` and the optional `SkyboxPipelineId`/`SkyboxBindGroup`, and after the opaque and alpha-mask phases it resolves the specialized pipeline from the `PipelineServer` and draws the fullscreen triangle with both dynamic offsets, exactly as Bevy does (`main_opaque_pass_3d_node.rs:119-133`); the node's doc comment no longer records the skybox input as missing. The only C20 work left is the visual example. **A defect in the port's own shader, found and fixed by that example (2026-10).** `render/examples/header/skybox.cpp` builds a six-face cubemap whose faces have distinct colours, spawns a `Camera3d` carrying `Skybox`, and orbits the origin while looking at it; the first run rendered a flat grey. The evidence localized it: the draw wiring was complete (a probe showed `SkyboxPipelineId`, `SkyboxBindGroup` and `RenderAssets<GpuImage>` resolving the handle to an 8x8x6 image), a hardcoded ray direction collapsed the frame to a single colour over all 14,400 sampled pixels, frames a second apart were byte-identical so camera motion never reached the output, and the observed greys were exactly the faces' *red* channels - `(10,20,30)` rendered `#0A0A0A` and `(70,200,210)` rendered `#464646`. **Cause: the shader declared `TextureCube<float>`.** In HLSL and Slang a sampled texture's element type is the *sampled* type, so `TextureCube<float>` samples to a scalar (the red channel) and using it as a colour broadcasts R over RGBA; WGSL has no scalar texture (`texture_cube<f32>` samples to `vec4<f32>`), so the faithful translation is `TextureCube<float4>`. Nothing was wrong in the image or render path - `Image::create2d_array` does validate `w*h*layers*pixel_size(format)` and copy the requested format (`image.hpp:1278-1287`), and the cube view comes from the image's own view descriptor as intended; the audit's earlier suspicion of the image module was wrong. With the element type fixed the example renders the six faces (green -X, magenta +Z, cyan -Z, yellow -Y all observed), the environment rotates as the camera orbits with clean face boundaries, no seams and no axis flips, and captures at successive orbit positions differ as they should. A capture was inspected for the two frames. **Remaining C20 work - the prepass variant - is blocked on the motion-vector prepass, and the dependency is now pinned (2026-10).** Bevy's `skybox/prepass.rs` is the *motion-vector* skybox: it writes the skybox's motion vectors into the prepass targets so TAA and motion blur can treat it correctly, and it draws only for views that carry both `Skybox` and `MotionVectorPrepass`. Its bind group is `(ViewUniform, PreviousViewData)` and its fragment targets come from `prepass_target_descriptors(normal_prepass, true, false)`. A tree-wide search finds **none** of `PreviousViewUniforms`, `PreviousViewData` or `prepass_target_descriptors` in Epix, so the variant cannot be ported on its own - this is the same gap the engine's own `LatePrepassNode` records as C14-D2 ("`PreviousViewUniformOffset` has no producer"). It also has no consumer here yet: Epix has no TAA and no motion blur, so a motion-vector skybox would write targets nothing reads, exactly the situation the tracker marks deferred elsewhere. It therefore lands together with the motion-vector prepass support (previous-view uniforms plus the prepass target descriptor helper), not before it. **Where that support belongs is now pinned too (2026-10):** in Bevy, `PreviousViewData` is *defined* in core-pipeline territory (`bevy_core_pipeline/src/prepass/mod.rs:96`, alongside `PreviousViewUniforms` and `PreviousViewUniformOffset`) but *written* by the PBR prepass (`bevy_pbr/src/prepass/mod.rs:198` plus its `prepare_previous_view_uniforms`), which is why the gap reads as PBR-shaped; Epix's prepass nodes live in `core_graph` instead, so the writer can be ported there from the same extracted-view matrices the prepass already uses, without waiting for the PBR port. `prepass_target_descriptors` (`prepass/mod.rs:383-405`, the four optional normal / motion-vector / deferred / deferred-lighting-id targets) is likewise core-pipeline and portable as-is. **The portable half has landed (2026-10):** `core_graph/include/epix/core_graph/prepass.hpp` + `src/prepass.cpp` now carry `PreviousViewData` (Bevy's five matrices in Bevy's order, with the `ShaderTypeInfo` opt-in the engine's uniform machinery needs), `PreviousViewUniforms`, `PreviousViewUniformOffset` and `prepass_target_descriptors`, with four tests pinning the 320-byte layout and matrix offsets, the offset default and the target set for every prepass combination including Bevy's skybox choice (normals plus motion vectors, never deferred). **What remains for C20's prepass variant:** the writer that fills `PreviousViewUniforms` from the extracted views (Bevy's `prepare_previous_view_uniforms`, which lives in the PBR prepass but belongs in `core_graph` here), the `skybox_prepass.wgsl` Slang port with `SkyboxPrepassPipeline`/`SkyboxPrepassPipelineKey` and its two prepare systems, and the draw in the prepass node for views carrying `Skybox` plus `MotionVectorPrepass`. **Those four pieces are fully specified now (2026-10), so the port is mechanical:** (1) the shader is Bevy's `skybox_prepass.wgsl` - group 0 binding 0 is the view and binding 1 the previous view, both dynamic uniform buffers, and the fragment takes the fullscreen vertex shader's `uv` (Epix's `core_pipeline/fullscreen_vertex.slang` already exports `float2 uv` at `vk::location(0)` with entry `fullscreen_vertex_shader`, `plugin.cpp:16-34`), converts `uv_to_ndc` as `uv * float2(2,-2) + float2(-1,1)`, takes the far-plane world position through `view.world_from_clip`, the previous clip position through `previous_view.clip_from_world`, and writes `(clip_pos - prev_clip_pos) * float2(0.5,-0.5)` to `SV_Target1`, which is the motion-vector slot `prepass_target_descriptors` already returns second; (2) the pipeline is the fullscreen vertex state with the depth state of the main skybox minus the colour attachment - `CORE_3D_DEPTH_FORMAT`, no depth write, `eGreaterEqual` - multisampled by the key; (3) the key is Bevy's `{samples, normal_prepass}`, since the target set is `prepass_target_descriptors(normal_prepass, true, false)`; (4) the draw belongs in Epix's early/late prepass node beside the phase items, and needs `ViewPrepassTextures` to carry a motion-vector attachment. **Two corrections found while attempting the port (2026-10):** the motion-vector target side already exists - `NormalPrepass`/`MotionVectorPrepass` are in `core3d.hpp:45,50`, `ViewPrepassTextures` carries `motion_vectors` with `motion_vectors_view()` (`:66-88`), and the prepass node already attaches it (`core3d.cpp:547,920`) - so only the previous-view *producer* was missing and the item is less blocked than this row implied. The attempt also hit a structural obstacle to settle before retrying: **`skybox.hpp` cannot include `core3d.hpp` or `prepass.hpp`**, because both include `skybox.hpp` (the cycle removed in round 35), so either the markers and `PreviousViewUniforms` are same-module forward declarations or the prepass declarations move into `core3d.hpp`, which already includes this header. Two API details must be read rather than guessed, since guessing cost a round: how `SkyboxPipeline::create` builds its `bind_group_layout` (copy that construction verbatim) and the multi-marker query shape - `ecs::Query` takes exactly one data and one filter, so markers belong in the data as `ecs::Has<T>` items beside a single `ecs::With<Skybox>`, exactly as `core3d.cpp:653-657` writes it. **C20 part 2 landed 2026-10:** the `skybox.wgsl` Slang port (the `View` import, Bevy's `coords_to_viewport_uv` inlined since Epix has no PBR utility library, the `skybox_vertex` triangle built from `SV_VulkanVertexID`, and `skybox_fragment` sampling the cube with the negated z and the brightness scale) plus `SkyboxPlugin`, which registers and loads the embedded shader, initializes `SpecializedRenderPipelines<SkyboxPipeline>` and builds the pipeline in `RenderStartup` as Bevy's `init_skybox_pipeline` does - and which `Core3dPlugin` now installs in the same place Bevy does (`core_3d/mod.rs:150`).
