One Epix API difference to translate: `ExtractComponent<C>::Out` is a *single* component (`render/render/include/epix/render/extract.hpp:57-61`), so Bevy's `(Skybox, SkyboxUniforms)` extraction pair becomes an extraction of `Skybox` plus a render-world system that inserts `SkyboxUniforms` - `Exposure` exists (`camera/include/epix/camera/exposure.hpp`) for the brightness term. |
| C20a | Fragment targets cannot express an unwritten output location | `render::FragmentState::targets` | ✅ | **Render-module mismatch found while porting C20's prepass skybox (2026-10).** Bevy's `FragmentState::targets` is `Vec<Option<ColorTargetState>>` (`bevy_render/src/render_resource/pipeline.rs:167`), where a `None` entry means the shader does not write that output location; Epix's is `std::vector<wgpu::ColorTargetState>` (`render/render/include/epix/render/pipeline.hpp:117`), and the conversion passes it straight through (`pipeline_server.cpp:313`). Bevy depends on the holes: its prepass target set is `[normal, motion_vector, deferred, deferred_lighting_id]` with a `None` wherever that prepass is off (`prepass/mod.rs:383-405`, which Epix now mirrors in `core_graph/prepass.hpp`), and the prepass skybox writes `@location(1)`, so its pipeline must declare target 0 as unwritten or the shader's output location cannot be bound. Epix has never needed a hole - every `FragmentState` in the tree writes its first target only (`skybox.cpp:121`, `tonemapping.cpp:491`, `blit.cpp:59`) - which is why the mismatch went unnoticed until a port actually needed one. Fixing it is a render-module change: `targets` becomes optional entries and `add_target` keeps pushing a written one, plus the conversion at `pipeline_server.cpp:313` must be verified against the vendored `webgpu.hpp` `FragmentState::targets` type, since whether the C++ wrapper can express a null entry is the open question and the first thing to check. Blocks C20's prepass skybox and any faithful port of Bevy's prepass or deferred pipelines. **Wrapper investigation (2026-10), which settles the shape of the fix:** the vendored C++ wrapper cannot hold a hole - `FragmentState::targets` is `SmallVec<wgpu::ColorTargetState>` and `setTargets` requires a range whose value type converts to `wgpu::ColorTargetState` (`build/<preset>/generated/webgpu/webgpu.hpp:5882-5890`), with no `std::optional` anywhere - and neither can the C ABI, whose `WGPUFragmentState::targets` is `WGPUColorTargetState const *` beside `targetCount` (`_deps/wgpu-.../include/webgpu/webgpu.h:4794-4801`), an array of *values*, so there is no null-entry spelling at either level. The only ABI-compatible encoding is therefore a sentinel entry whose format is `Undefined`, which is how a C shim has to map wgpu's Rust `&[Option<ColorTargetState>]` onto an array of values. So the fix likely needs **no** change to the render module's types: an unwritten slot is expressed as `wgpu::ColorTargetState().setFormat(wgpu::TextureFormat::eUndefined)`, which `setTargets` already accepts, and the decisive check is empirical - queue a pipeline whose target 0 is that sentinel while the shader writes `@location(1)` and see whether wgpu accepts it. If it does, C20a collapses into a documented convention plus a helper; if it does not, the fallback is bypassing the wrapper at `pipeline_server.cpp:313` by hand-building the `WGPUFragmentState` with a null or sentinel entry, which is the only remaining option and would be a render-module change needing approval. **Resolved 2026-10, and no render-module change was needed:** the sentinel *is* accepted - `SkyboxPrepass.UndefinedFormatTargetIsAcceptedAsAnUnwrittenSlot` (`core_graph/tests/header/skybox_tests.cpp`) queues a pipeline whose target 0 has `Undefined` format while its shader writes `SV_Target1`, and wgpu compiles it. So C20a is an Epix *convention*, not a divergence in behaviour: `prepass_target_descriptors` stays Bevy-shaped (`std::array<std::optional<ColorTargetState>, 4>`) and `prepass_pipeline_targets` (`core_graph/include/epix/core_graph/prepass.hpp`) converts it into the value list a fragment stage can carry, substituting an `Undefined`-format target for each unwritten location, with tests pinning both the skybox set (holes at 2 and 3) and the deferred set (holes at 0 and 1). The probe test is the regression guard, since it is the only thing that proves the sentinel is legal rather than merely expressible. |
| C20b | Skybox prepass draw chain | early/late prepass node | ✅ | **The variant's pipeline, shader and per-view data are ported (commit `e93c27b2`); this row is the one chain left before it draws.** Bevy's draw is `prepass/node.rs:215-235`: after the prepass phase items, for a view having `RenderSkyboxPrepassPipeline`, `SkyboxPrepassBindGroup` *and* `PreviousViewUniformOffset`, it sets the pipeline from the cache, binds group 0 with **two** dynamic offsets `[view_uniform_offset.offset, view_prev_uniform_offset.offset]` (which is why the layout declares both buffers dynamic), and draws `(0..3, 0..1)` with the fullscreen triangle. That condition is the chain: (a) a main-world producer for `PreviousViewData` (Bevy `update_previous_view_data`, `bevy_pbr/src/prepass/mod.rs:189-206`, storing the *current* camera matrices to be read one frame late); (b) extraction of that component into the render world (Bevy copies it with the camera, Epix can register `ExtractComponentPlugin<PreviousViewData>` from core_graph); (c) `prepare_previous_view_uniforms`, already ported, registered in `Prepare` so each view is stamped with its `PreviousViewUniformOffset`; (d) the draw itself beside the phase items. Without (a) and (b) no offset exists, so (d) would compile and then silently never draw - which is why the draw is not worth wiring in isolation. **Completed and behaviour-verified 2026-10** (commits `e93c27b2`, `ce9d3e1d`, `b90c24af`, `215cdbf6`): the shader, pipeline, bind group and previous-view uniforms are prepared per view, and the draw runs in the prepass node with both dynamic offsets bound. `render/examples/header/skybox.cpp` now carries `DepthPrepass`/`NormalPrepass`/`MotionVectorPrepass` and reports the draw's four preconditions - `pipeline=true bind_group=true view_offset=true previous_offset=true` - while the frame still shows all six faces and stdout carries no validation errors. Note the verification trap this row records: the first probe reading (`bind_group=false previous_offset=false`) was an artifact of a one-shot probe with no defined position in the render schedule, and it was briefly read as a real failure; the final reading comes from a probe that waits for frame 30 and is ordered after `prepare_skybox_prepass_bind_groups`. |
| C22 | The `bevy_pbr` prerequisite chain behind the mesh view layout | Epix's `pbr` module holds the key value types only (`MeshPipelineKey`, `MeshPipelineViewLayoutKey` with `OIT_ENABLED` included) | 🚫 | **Sized 2026-10, which corrects how C18, B39, C14-D2 and M8e have been described.** The mesh view bind group Bevy's mesh pipeline and OIT binding set is `@group(0)` with **32 bindings** (`bevy_pbr/src/render/mesh_view_bindings.wgsl`): view, `GpuLights`, point and directional shadow textures plus comparison and linear samplers, three clustering buffers, globals, fog, light probes, visibility ranges, SSR settings, the SSAO texture, the environment map uniform, the atmosphere LUTs, the three prepass textures, the deferred prepass texture, the view transmission pair, and OIT's `oit_layers`/`oit_layer_ids`/`oit_settings` at bindings 26-28. **What the remaining work turns on is still absent**: the `MeshPipelineViewLayout` itself (the layout with those 32 entries) and `MeshViewBindGroup` have no definition in the tree - only the *key* types exist (`MeshPipelineViewLayoutKey` and `MeshPipelineViewLayoutKeyValue` in `mesh_pipeline_key.hpp`, plus `MeshPipelineKey`), which is why a substring search for the layout's name is not evidence either way. **Progress since (2026-10):** the chain's data layer is landed and pinned - `GpuLights` and its assembly, `LightMeta` and its uniform offsets, the cluster buffers/bindings/extraction/assignment, light counts and sort keys, the light flags and packing, the shared reverse-Z projection, the shadow phase item with its keys, the shadow texture and view budgets, the directional slice assignment, the cascade chain (bounds to corners to cascades to view requests), the point-light face rotations and cubemap frusta, `Frustum::NEAR_PLANE_IDX` (see C23), and Bevy's light unit tables. What remains is system-shaped: `prepare_lights` creating the view entities and depth attachments, the shadow specialization and queue, the pass node, then `mesh_view_bindings.rs` and `mesh.rs`/`MeshPipeline` themselves. The chain is `mesh.rs` (3143 lines), `light.rs` (2160), `gpu_preprocess.rs` (2531), `mesh_view_bindings.rs` (807), `skin.rs` (568), `mesh_bindings.rs` (525), `morph.rs` (137), `fog.rs` (133) plus the WGSL and the clustering/atmosphere/environment/SSR/SSAO pieces: **`bevy_pbr` is 32,896 lines over 53 files**, against Epix's whole render module at 34,757. Bevy's layout does vary by `MeshPipelineViewLayoutKey`, so a reduced-feature layout is Bevy-shaped in principle, but the mesh and OIT shaders read the full set, so a faithful port is the chain rather than a subset. **Systems landed (2026-10):** `pbr/src/light_systems.cpp` holds `update_point_light_frusta`, `update_directional_light_frusta`, `clear_directional_light_cascades` and `build_directional_light_cascades`, each verified by a World-driven test rather than only compiled, alongside the clusterable-object assignment-data constructors. They are registered by tests alone **by decision**: Bevy's `PbrPlugin` also builds the mesh pipeline, the shadow pass, OIT and the cluster assignment, so a lights-only plugin under that name would be a capability divergence - it lands once it can register Bevy's real set. **The scheduling they will need, taken from Bevy's own plugin (`bevy_light/src/lib.rs:148-200`):** `validate_shadow_map_size` before `build_directional_light_cascades`; `add_clusters` in `AddClusters` after `CameraUpdateSystems`; `assign_objects_to_clusters` in `AssignLightsToClusters` after `TransformSystems::Propagate`, `VisibilitySystems::CheckVisibility` and `CameraUpdateSystems`; `clear_directional_light_cascades` in `UpdateDirectionalLightCascades` after propagate and camera; the three frusta systems in `UpdateLightFrusta`, the directional one after `UpdateDirectionalLightCascades` and visibility, the point and spot ones after `AssignLightsToClusters` - which is exactly what their gate on the global clustered set requires - with point and spot declared `ambiguous_with` each other because no entity is both a point and a spot light; then `check_dir_light_mesh_visibility` and `check_point_light_mesh_visibility` in `CheckLightVisibility` after `CalculateBounds` and the frusta, which is where lights mark shadow casters visible even when no camera sees them. **Still absent:** `assign_objects_to_clusters`'s orchestration (its internals and data construction are ported), `prepare_lights`, `MeshViewBindGroup` and the layout, `MeshPipeline`, the shadow specialization and queue, and the shadow pass node. |
| C23 | `Frustum::NEAR_PLANE_IDX`, the plane index Bevy makes public | missing: Epix's `Frustum` had no such constant, while Bevy declares it inside `impl Frustum` as `pub const NEAR_PLANE_IDX: usize = 4` (`bevy_camera/src/primitives.rs:293`), beside a *private* `FAR_PLANE_IDX = 5` and a private `INACTIVE_HALF_SPACE` | FIXED | **Landed 2026-10 (commit `cb9884b6`).** Bevy needs the index in `intersects_sphere` and in the directional-cascade push that sets a cascade view's near plane to infinity; Epix's `directional_view_frustum` needs it for the same reason. Comparing the two `Frustum` types item by item showed this was the *only* public-surface difference: the plane derivation matches exactly (left `row3 + row0`, right `row3 - row0`, top `row3 + row1`, bottom `row3 - row1`, near `row3 + row2`, far `row2`), and `from_clip_from_world`, `from_clip_from_world_custom_far`, `intersects_sphere`, `intersects_obb`, `intersects_obb_identity` and `contains_aabb` all match, with Epix's plane initializer already filling Bevy's inactive value. The camera module gained the constant and nothing else. Worth knowing for anyone who reads the push that uses it: under the engine's zero-to-one depth convention that slot is **inert** - `row3 + row2` is the negative-one-to-one near plane, which admits every point in front of the view, and the true bound `row3 - row2` is never computed because the GPU clips there anyway. The push is faithful and changes no culling, which is why a test asserting it frees a culled sphere failed and had to be corrected to assert the equality instead. |
| C21 | Shared view shader module namespace follows its import path (`bevy_render::view::{View, ColorGrading}`) | Slang module `epix.view` | ✅| The Slang module now exports `View` and `ColorGrading` from `epix::view`, matching Bevy's shader-module namespace and Epix's WGSL `epix::view` import path. Mesh, sprite, text, and tonemapping use the qualified names consistently. A real PipelineServer regression compiles an importing Slang entry point; all affected visual pipelines were captured across three stable frames. |

## Core 3D Intentional Divergences

| ID | Bevy behavior | Epix adaptation | Rationale |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C13-D1 | `Core3dPlugin` installs real prepass/deferred nodes (`EarlyPrepassNode`, `LatePrepassNode`, `EarlyDeferredGBufferPrepassNode`, `LateDeferredGBufferPrepassNode`, `CopyDeferredLightingIdNode`). | **Closed:** all five nodes are real (`CopyDeferredLightingIdNode` since C15-D4), so no `EmptyNode` remains in the Core3D chain. | The extension points and edge order were preserved throughout, which is why each subsystem could be filled without touching Core3D. |
| C13-D2 | `Core3dPlugin` also installs `SkyboxPlugin`, and the opaque node draws a skybox from `SkyboxPipelineId`/`SkyboxBindGroup`. | No skybox inputs on `MainOpaquePass3dNode`. | Epix has no skybox subsystem; the view query omits the two optional skybox components instead of inventing placeholder state. |
| C13-D3 | `add_systems(PostUpdate, check_msaa)` disables MSAA for deferred views. | Implemented by C15. | **Closed:** `check_msaa` queries `Mut<Msaa>` with `With<Camera, DeferredPrepass>`, warns with Bevy's message and forces `Msaa::Off`, and `Core3dPlugin` registers it in the main world's `PostUpdate`. A regression test asserts a `Sample4` deferred view becomes `Off`, an already-`Off` deferred view stays `Off`, and a non-deferred `Sample4` view is untouched. |
| C13-D4 | `configure_occlusion_culling_view_targets` adds `TEXTURE_BINDING` to the depth usage. | Not registered. | Requires the occlusion-culling subsystem that also blocks `LatePrepassNode`/`LateDeferredGBufferPrepassNode` (C14-D2/C15-D2); the system is registered by Bevy's occlusion-culling plugin, not by `Core3dPlugin`. |
| C13-D5 | `prepare_core_3d_depth_textures` adds `COPY_SRC` when a `DepthPrepass` is present. | Implemented by C14. | **Closed:** the query reads `Has<DepthPrepass>` and unions `TextureUsage::eCopySrc` into the shared per-target depth usage, asserted by a regression test. |
| C13-D6 | `extract_camera_prepass_phase` and `prepare_prepass_textures`. | Implemented by C14, with the deferred half completed by C15. | **Closed:** both exist with Bevy's names and signatures and run in the schedules Bevy uses (`ExtractSchedule` and `RenderSystems::PrepareResources`). Round 7 merged the deferred phases into the *one* system Bevy has and added the marker insert/remove Bevy performs on the synced render entity, so a real app's render-world camera now actually carries `DepthPrepass`/`NormalPrepass`/`MotionVectorPrepass`/`DeferredPrepass`. |
| C13-D7 | `OpaqueNoLightmap3dBatchSetKey`/`OpaqueNoLightmap3dBinKey` live in `bevy_core_pipeline::prepass`. | Declared in `core_3d.hpp` beside their first consumer. | One definition shared with C14 rather than a duplicate; names and fields are unchanged. |
| C13-D8 | `lightmap_slab: Option<NonMaxU32>` (and Bevy's other `NonMaxU32` identities). | `std::optional<std::uint32_t>`. | Epix has no `NonMaxU32`; the "never `u32::MAX`" invariant is not type-enforced. |
| C13-D9 | The 3D pass nodes honour `MainPassResolutionOverride` and consume `ViewUniformOffset`. | Nodes use `ExtractedCamera::viewport` only. | Epix's Core2D nodes take the same shape; the resolution-override divergence belongs to the render-module tracker. |
| C13-D10 | `split_range` is a lazy iterator and `assert!`s on empty input or zero splits. | `split_transmissive_range` returns a `std::vector` and throws `std::invalid_argument`. | The project convention is a typed exception instead of `panic!`/`assert!`; tests cover both failure modes. |
| C13-D11 | The transmissive node `.expect()`s `ViewTransmissionTexture`. | It returns without drawing when the prepared texture is missing. | Epix returns instead of aborting when a prepare system skipped the view. |
| C13-D12 | `SamplerDescriptor::default()` leaves `max_anisotropy` at 1. | `prepare_core_3d_transmission_textures` sets `maxAnisotropy(1)` explicitly. | wgpu-native's generated C++ descriptor does not initialize this required field, so the Rust default cannot be inherited (same adaptation as `BlitPipeline`'s sampler). |
| C13-D13 | `prepare_core_3d_depth_textures` `.expect()`s a usage entry for every queried view. | A view that is not a member of all four phases is skipped before any texture is created. | Bevy's `expect` encodes the same invariant (only phase members reach the usage map); Epix skips instead of panicking, matching `prepare_core_2d_depth_textures`. |

## Cross-module Integration Findings

| ID | Area | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1 | `TaskPool::Scope` completion and failure handling used by `RenderContext::finish` | ✅| FIXED | Long-running mesh rendering exposed a lost-wakeup race: scoped workers changed the atomic pending count without holding the condition-variable mutex, so the final notification could occur between the waiter's predicate check and sleep. The mixed protocol now uses `std::atomic::wait`/`notify_one`; task exceptions are captured and rethrown after all scoped work completes, matching Bevy's scoped-task failure propagation. |
| P2 | Empty `async_channel::recv` scheduling | REVIEW | DEFERRED (needs its own utils/asset integration audit) | The supplied active-worker stacks are IO-pool asset tasks repeatedly rescheduling an empty receive at `async_channel.hpp:300`, rather than threads blocked in the render failure. This busy polling consumes the IO workers and needs a separate parent `utils`/asset integration audit; suspending those workers after a render freeze did not unblock `RenderContext::finish`, so it is not the P1 stall's immediate cause. |
| P3 | LDR `ViewTarget` main-texture format | ✅| FIXED | The LDR main texture and its default view now use `RGBA8UnormSrgb`, matching Bevy 0.18's `TextureFormat::bevy_default()`; the linear twin remains an allowed alternate view format. A direct GPU regression checks both the component and underlying texture format. |
| P4 | Render-world `AssetServer` availability | ✅| FIXED | Render initialization clones the main-world `AssetServer` resource into the render world, matching Bevy's `initialize_render_app`. This enables render-startup systems such as `FullscreenMaterialPlugin` to resolve `ShaderRef::Path` without an invented source-registration API. |
| P5 | Render-only `Image` extraction | ✅| FIXED | `Image` now preserves Bevy's `Option<Vec<u8>>` present/absent distinction and `RenderAsset<Image>::take_gpu_data` transfers render-only pixels while retaining source metadata. Repeated extraction follows Bevy's `had_data`/previous-GPU-image rule, and prepare skips uploads only when the payload is absent—not merely empty. Image and render parity tests cover metadata retention, empty-present data, one-shot extraction, retry validity, and byte accounting. This parent-render fix is required for C16's render-only LUT assets. |
| P6 | Core3D phase dependency on mesh slab identity | ✅ | FIXED | **Re-audited 2026-09-17: this row was stale - the move is committed** (the 2026-09-08 audit-log entry records it). `render::mesh::{SlabId, MeshBufferSlice}` owns the generic value types at the same lower render-layer boundary as Bevy in `render/render/include/epix/render/mesh_allocator.hpp` (`MeshBufferSlice` at `:39`, `SlabId` at `:46`), and `epix_mesh` consumes them without aliases or duplicate Core3D types. `MeshBufferSlice` uses Bevy's single `range` shape. This removes the otherwise circular C13 dependency while keeping the allocator implementation in the mesh module; full clean build, focused tests and mesh/text visual verification passed at the time. |
| P7 | Render-phase command state and phase-draw locking: Bevy's `RenderCommandState` holds `SystemState<C::Param>` plus **separate** `QueryState<C::ViewQuery>`/`QueryState<C::ItemQuery>`, updates archetypes **only** in `prepare`, and reads the view/item through the `get_manual` family in `draw` (draw.rs:296-349); `SortedRenderPhase::render_range` holds `draw_functions.write()` for the whole loop (render_phase/mod.rs:1474-1489); and a command's `Param` is an **already-resolved** `SystemParamItem` — a tuple, or the bare parameter when there is only one | ✅ | FIXED | **An access violation in `QueryState::new_archetype_internal` (`state.hpp:324`) reproduced 2/20 runs of the single-camera `sprite_tonemapping` example and 6/6 of its multi-view-HDR variant, and the debugger stack ran `RenderCommandState::draw → params->get() → QueryState::query_with_ticks → update_archetypes → push_back`.** Epix had bundled the view query, the item query and the command's own parameters into one `ecs::ParamSet` and re-fetched all three on **every** draw; because Epix's `SystemParam<Query>::get_param` updates archetypes (`system_param.hpp:24-26`) where Bevy's does not, the eight viewports that record commands concurrently pushed into the same shared `QueryState` archetype vector — the draw state is created once per registered draw function and shared by every view and item. **Fix:** `RenderCommandState` now mirrors Bevy's three separate states (initialized and access-validated as one set, then held apart), `prepare` alone updates the view/entity archetypes and fetches the command parameters, `draw` reads view and item through `query_manual_with_ticks` (never mutating), the `RenderCommand` concept requires a read-only `Param` (Bevy's `C::Param: ReadOnlySystemParam`), and every render command receives its `Param` already resolved — the bare parameter for one, `std::tuple<...>` for several, `std::tuple<>` for none — instead of a lazy `ParamSet`. **Closed 2026-09-28:** `DrawFunctions` now matches Bevy's shape exactly - it owns a `utils::RwLock<DrawFunctionsInternal>` and exposes only `read()`/`write()`, the per-operation locks are gone, and both `SortedRenderPhase::render_range` and `BinnedRenderPhase::render` take one write guard for the whole pass (`prepare` plus every item's draw), threading the payload through the binned helpers as Bevy's `draw_functions.get_mut` does. Verified by build, CTest 85/85, and byte-identical captures of sprite_tonemapping, mesh_rendering and sprite_basic. Verified: render parity **153/153**, sprite-render parity **8/8**, text embedded-shader **1/1**, full CTest **79/79**, and 20+10+40 launches of the example all alive. |
| P8 | `ParamSet` handle shape: Bevy's `ParamSet` holds `param_states: &'s mut T::State` and exposes exactly one member at a time through `p0()..p7()`, each taking `&mut self` ("No other parameters may be accessed while this one is active", system_param.rs:635-744); conflicts *between* members are allowed precisely because two items can never be live together | ✅ implemented | FIXED (residual is a language limit) | **Fixed 2026-09-17.** Epix's `ParamSet` had raw `State*`/`World*`/`const SystemMeta*` members **and** an all-members `get()` returning every item as a tuple, so conflicting members (including a read and a write of the same component type) could be live simultaneously; `render.cpp:452-454` already relied on that with `ParamSet<World&, ResMut<Schedules>>`. The accessors are now `get<I>(invocable)` only - the member's item lives for the duration of the callback - plus a const overload restricted to read-only members, and the tuple-returning/value-returning forms are gone. The three systems that genuinely needed `World&` plus a resource were restructured the way Bevy expresses them: the app's state-update system and the render extract-commands system now use `World::resource_scope`, and `AssetServer::handle_internal_events` takes `ecs::World&` and copies the server handle instead of borrowing the resource map. Nine example call sites were migrated to per-index callbacks (`pixel_body_sand` ×6, `fallingsand` ×2 sites in both header and module twins, `text_interactive` ×2 systems in both twins; the text interaction system now reads the camera-derived world cursor before taking the Transform-writing query). New coverage: `ecs/tests/header/param_set.cpp`. **Remaining, recorded rather than claimed:** the raw-pointer members give no lifetime or exclusive-borrow enforcement (a C++ limitation, as the user noted), and nesting `get<I>` callbacks can still re-create simultaneous access - the API now makes the one-at-a-time intent explicit but cannot enforce it. The two-pass `init_access` (members checked against a clone, then merged) continues to match Bevy (param.hpp:318-342). |
| P9 | Descriptor-based bind group layouts and interning: Bevy's `RenderPipelineDescriptor::layout` holds `Vec<BindGroupLayoutDescriptor>` and `PipelineCache` interns them into native layouts through its private `BindGroupLayoutCache`; every Epix pipeline instead stores native `wgpu::BindGroupLayout` handles created eagerly | ✅ | FIXED (view-binding holder tracked as P10) | **Open, approved 2026-09-17 (S9 mesh2d work); mechanism corrected and audit completed.** Found while porting `Mesh2dPipeline`: Bevy's `Mesh2dPipeline { view_layout, mesh_layout }` holds `BindGroupLayoutDescriptor`s and the cache resolves them at specialization time, whereas Epix's `RenderPipelineDescriptor::layouts` is `std::vector<wgpu::BindGroupLayout>` (native handles) and there is no bind group layout interning anywhere - `blit.cpp:101`, `copy_deferred_lighting_id.cpp:59`, `tonemapping.cpp:717`, `core3d_prepass.cpp:225`, `fullscreen_material.hpp:266` and the sprite pipeline all call `device.createBindGroupLayout` directly. `LayoutCache` (`pipeline_server.hpp:79-120`) caches *pipeline* layouts keyed by the constituent layout ids, which is the half Epix already has. **Correction:** this row originally named the interning cache `ShaderBindGroupLayouts`; that type has **zero** matches in pinned 0.18. The real mechanism is `BindGroupLayoutDescriptor { label: Cow<str>, entries: Vec<BindGroupLayoutEntry> }` with derived `Hash`/`Eq` (`render_resource/pipeline.rs:90-104`), `RenderPipelineDescriptor::layout: Vec<BindGroupLayoutDescriptor>` (`pipeline.rs:112`) and the private `BindGroupLayoutCache { bgls: HashMap<BindGroupLayoutDescriptor, BindGroupLayout> }` with `get(render_device, descriptor) -> BindGroupLayout` (`render_resource/pipeline_cache.rs:211-220`); `LayoutCache` is `pipeline_cache.rs:121-144`. Bevy exposes interning publicly as `PipelineCache::get_bind_group_layout(&descriptor)`, which ~14 of its own passes call to build bind groups. **Migration surface measured:** 66 `device.createBindGroupLayout(` sites over 23 files, 10 `RenderPipelineDescriptor` construction sites, two `bind_group_layout<C>` helper uses. Plan: (1) add the descriptor type plus the cache and its public accessor - additive, existing native-handle paths keep working; (2) move `RenderPipelineDescriptor::layouts` onto descriptors and resolve them in `process_pipeline` through the cache; (3) migrate the 66 call sites, render internals first and examples/tests after; (4) adopt descriptors in `Mesh2dPipeline`, which is what this row was opened for. Cross-cutting: it changes `RenderPipelineDescriptor` and therefore touches every pipeline in the engine, so each step needs its own tracked batch and approval. **Progress (2026-09-17):** step 1 (`7b767517`, refined by `5d3b3e50`) added the descriptor type, the interning cache and its public accessor. Step 2a is now **complete for the engine**: sprite (`30642eac`), mesh2d's legacy path and text (`00392822`), and blit, tonemapping, copy_deferred_lighting_id, fullscreen_material, screenshot and `ViewUniformBindingLayout` (`d72ad56b`) all obtain their layouts through `get_bind_group_layout`, so no engine pipeline calls `device.createBindGroupLayout` any more and each pipeline shares one interned native layout per descriptor with the bind groups it is used with. `AsBindGroup::bind_group_layout` needs **no** change: Bevy's own trait default creates directly from `bind_group_layout_descriptor` (`render_resource/bind_group.rs`), so Epix's `bind_group_layout<C>(device)` already matches it, and `AsBindGroup<C>::layout_entries()` is the building block for the descriptor form, which arrives with S10c's material path. The remaining sites that both create layouts *and* feed engine pipeline descriptors are the four path-tracer examples and `liquid_eulerian` (header and module); they fold into step 2b, where the descriptor field changes anyway, rather than being converted twice. Raw-wgpu example and test sites (`binned_phase_geometry`, `core3d_prepass`, `grid_gpu/shader_test`, `render_smoke_test`, the mesh2d shader test's fallback layout) never enter the engine's pipeline cache, so interning does not apply to them. **Step 2b landed 2026-09-17:** `RenderPipelineDescriptor::layouts` and `ComputePipelineDescriptor::layouts` now hold `std::vector<BindGroupLayoutDescriptor>`, and `process_pipeline` interns them through the bind-group-layout cache before building the pipeline layout (`pipeline_cache.rs:519-523`). Every layout holder Bevy models as a descriptor now stores one (`BlitPipeline`, `TonemappingPipeline`, `ScreenshotBlitPipeline`, `FullscreenMaterialPipeline`, `CopyDeferredLightingIdPipeline` and `Mesh2dPipeline` - the last being what this row was opened for), the `create_bind_group` helpers take the pipeline cache as Bevy's do, and the example pipelines were converted in the same pass. **Closed 2026-09-23:** the last redundant interned layouts are gone - `SpritePipeline`, `Text2dPipelineCache` and the legacy `Mesh2dPipelineCache` now hold descriptors only and fetch the interned layout where their bind groups are created, as Bevy's passes do. P9's substance is in place: descriptors in both pipeline descriptors, interning in `process_pipeline`, and `Mesh2dPipeline` holding descriptors. The one remaining Epix-only layout holder is the view binding, tracked as P10 and resolved with S9 step 3. |
| P10 | View uniform binding: Bevy's `prepare_view_uniforms` writes the dynamic uniform buffer and inserts a per-view `ViewUniformOffset` (`bevy_render/src/view/mod.rs:910-998`) and creates **no bind group at all** - each pipeline binds group 0 from its own layout in its draw command | `ViewUniforms` + per-view `ViewUniformOffset`; each pipeline creates its own group-0 bind group | ✅ | **Fixed 2026-10.** Epix's `create_uniform_for_view` (its `prepare_view_uniforms`) wrote the dynamic uniform buffer and inserted `ViewUniformOffset` like Bevy, but then additionally created an Epix-only `ViewBindGroup` component from an Epix-only `ViewUniformBindingLayout` resource (zero matches in pinned 0.18). Nothing consumed it: its only user was the `BindViewUniform` render command, which no pipeline registered, while every drawing pipeline already built its own group 0 from its own layout exactly as Bevy does (`prepare_mesh2d_view_bind_groups` -> `SetMesh2dViewBindGroup<0>`, `prepare_sprite_view_bind_groups` -> `SetSpriteViewBindGroup<0>`, and the core-graph post passes). `ViewBindGroup`, `ViewUniformBindingLayout`, `BindViewUniform` and `view_uniform_layout_descriptor()` are deleted, leaving the Bevy shape: central uniform buffer + offsets, per-pipeline bind groups. The `ViewPlugin` test that asserted the Epix-only resource's absence now asserts that neither `ViewUniforms` nor `ViewTargetAttachments` leaks into the main world when `ViewPlugin` is attached without a render sub-app. Verified: build clean, ctest 87/87 in 51.86 s, 60-example sweep clean except the pre-existing `fallingsand_falling_sand`, and captures inspected for the three bind-group paths - `sprite_basic` (sprite pipeline), `sprite_render_tilemap_chunk` (mesh2d material/opaque phases) and `render_glfw_tonemapping` (the eight fullscreen tonemapping tiles). |


## Build Hygiene (not a Bevy parity item)

| ID | Rule | Evidence and consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| H1 | When a header change looks like it did not propagate, do a full clean rebuild; do not treat the incremental state as trustworthy. | The observed symptom is precise and was reproduced several times: `ninja -t deps <obj>` reports `#deps 0` (or stops reporting the object at all), `cmake --build --target epix_render` answers "no work to do" while a header it includes is newer, and an incremental build after a header edit leaves consumers stale, producing a mixed-ABI binary (access violations, stack overflows, and one corrupt PNG test fixture in this effort). One contributing factor was removed on 2026-09-11: the root `CMakeLists.txt` had stripped `/showIncludes` from `CMAKE_DEPFILE_FLAGS_C`/`_CXX`, and after that removal the regenerated rules carry `/showIncludes` for both the scanned and unscanned compile rules, with a real dependency count recorded at least once (`#deps 1196` for one render translation unit). **That is not a complete explanation, and this row does not claim one:** dependency counts were later observed empty again after interrupted builds, so the remaining cause is inside Ninja/CMake's dep-log handling rather than in this repository, and chasing it is out of scope. The operative rule stays the empirical one: a clean rebuild is the only state that may be trusted after a header edit. The previously documented `b29_clean_build.bat` no longer exists; the supported no-modules equivalent is `cmake --build build/msvc-reldeb-no-modules --target clean --parallel 8` followed by `cmake --build build/msvc-reldeb-no-modules --parallel 8`. | GUIDANCE | |
| H2 | An example that adds `render::RenderPlugin` must also add the main-world `mesh::MeshPlugin`. | Every `bevy` app gets `Assets<Mesh>` through `DefaultPlugins`; Epix compositions assemble plugins by hand, and `RenderPlugin::attach` installs `render::mesh::MeshRenderAssetPlugin` exactly where Bevy's `RenderPlugin` installs `MeshRenderAssetPlugin` (`bevy_render/src/lib.rs:362-380`), so without `MeshPlugin` the render-side `extract_render_asset<Mesh>` fails resource validation on every frame (`parameter validation error ... Assets<Mesh> ... Resource does not exist`, 10,540 lines in one 11-second run). Fixed (2026-09-11) across the 19 affected examples —the render GLFW/imgui/sfml examples and the four experimental path tracers —by adding `#include <epix/mesh.hpp>` (or `import epix.mesh;`) plus `.add_plugins(mesh::MeshPlugin{})`. The four path tracers need the fully qualified `::epix::mesh::MeshPlugin{}`, because they have both `using namespace epix;` and `using namespace epix::render;` in scope and that makes `mesh::` ambiguous against `epix::render::mesh`; those files already qualify `::epix::camera::CameraPlugin` the same way. | GUIDANCE | |

| H3 | The module-preset mesh test twin (`epix_engine/mesh/tests/module/module_test.cpp`) is a parallel copy of `tests/header/module_test.cpp` and must remain synchronized even while module builds are unavailable. | **Updated 2026-09-14:** the stale claim that the twin had fallen behind is no longer true; its mesh tests, including B49a, M16/M21, B12/B25/B26/B31, and M20, are synchronized with the header source. The current compiler toolchains still cannot build the module configuration, so only the header source is executable evidence. Module compilation remains intentionally excluded, but every mesh API batch must update and mechanically compare both test blocks so unsupported builds do not become an excuse for source drift. | GUIDANCE | |

Earlier log entries describe what was claimed at that checkpoint. Revised rows
above supersede unsupported acceptance/parity claims; an implementation is not
an accepted divergence merely because it was previously committed.

| 2026-10 | **S11 closed: `Wireframe2dPlugin` is ported, so the last missing facility of the audited `sprite` module exists.** Bevy's 2D wireframe is a self-contained binned-phase facility (`bevy_sprite_render/src/mesh2d/wireframe2d.rs`): `Wireframe2dPhaseItem` is a `BinnedPhaseItem` whose `draw_function()` and `cached_pipeline()` come from the **batch-set** key (`:186`, `:214`), `Wireframe2dBatchSetKey` orders `(pipeline, wireframe-material asset, draw function, vertex slab, index slab)` with `indexed() = index_slab.is_some()` (`:240-266`), `Wireframe2dBinKey` holds the **mesh** asset (`:268-275`), and `Wireframe2dPipeline::specialize` derives everything from `Mesh2dPipeline::specialize` and changes exactly five things: the label, a `PushConstantRange { FRAGMENT, 0..16 }`, the fragment shader (the 12-line `wireframe2d.wgsl`, whose vertex stage stays mesh2d's and which returns `push_constants.color`), `primitive.polygon_mode = PolygonMode::Line`, and `depth_stencil.bias.slope_scale = 1.0` (`:336-356`). **No wireframe mesh is generated** -- the original mesh is drawn with polygon-mode lines, exactly as Bevy's 3D wireframe does. The draw sequence is `SetItemPipeline, SetMesh2dViewBindGroup<0>, SetMesh2dBindGroup<1>, SetWireframe2dPushConstants, DrawMesh2d` (`:311-317`), the node runs between `Node2d::EndMainPass` and `Node2d::PostProcessing` (`:121-129`), and the plugin owns `Wireframe2d`, `NoWireframe2d`, `Wireframe2dColor`, `Wireframe2dConfig { global, default_color }`, `Mesh2dWireframe`, `Wireframe2dMaterial` -> `RenderWireframeMaterial` (via `RenderAsset`), five specialization caches, the global material and twelve systems with Bevy's schedules and skip order. Bevy's `SpriteRenderPlugin` does **not** add this plugin (`lib.rs:61-121`), so applications opt in. | **Ported into `sprite_render` (`include/epix/sprite_render/wireframe2d.hpp`, `src/wireframe2d.cpp`) plus a shader registered as `embedded://sprite/wireframe2d.slang`, and exercised by a new example (`sprite/examples/wireframe2d.cpp`).** The colour is Bevy's real 16-byte **push constant**: with the engine back on SPIR-V (previous row) the fragment shader compiles its `[[vk::push_constant]]` block to a genuine `PushConstant` variable, the pipeline layout publishes `immediateSize` through `PushConstantRange`, and `SetWireframe2dPushConstants` writes it with `setImmediates(0, ...)`. Epix-specific mappings: `PolygonMode` is a wgpu-native primitive-state extension chained as `PrimitiveStateExtras` (the descriptor owns the chain), the draw-function id is stored in a `Wireframe2dDrawFunction` resource because the command sequence's type cannot be named through `app_add_render_commands` (the S10f convention), `Mesh2dWireframe` gets an `AsAssetId` specialization so `AssetChanged<Mesh2dWireframe>` drives re-specialization, and colours are linear `glm::vec4` because Epix has no `bevy_color`. **Verification:** full build green; `ctest` **86/86 in 24.89 s** (the six new wireframe tests included); the 59-example sweep is **58 clean** with only the pre-existing `fallingsand_falling_sand` material2d error; the new example's capture shows all three paths at once -- a grey global wireframe over the triangle, a red `Wireframe2dColor` wireframe over the box (including its tessellation diagonal, which is what polygon-mode lines produce), and none on the `NoWireframe2d` circle -- with **0 stderr bytes** and an empty error scan. The example and test twins for the module preset are written (`sprite/examples/module/wireframe2d.cpp`; `sprite_render/tests` has no module directory, so its test needs no twin), and remain unverified only because this machine cannot build the module configuration. |
| 2026-10 | **The temporary WGSL detour is over: the engine compiles Slang to SPIR-V again, and the whole reason it could not is three shader builtins.** Slang declares the `DrawParameters` capability only because the HLSL index builtins are D3D-semantics ones that exclude the base vertex/instance: `SV_VertexID`/`SV_InstanceID` make it read `BaseVertex`/`BaseInstance` (probe `spvasm`: `OpCapability DrawParameters` + those two `BuiltIn`s). Slang's Vulkan builtins read the raw builtins instead — `SV_VulkanVertexID`/`SV_VulkanInstanceID` emit `OpCapability Shader` alone with `BuiltIn VertexIndex`/`BuiltIn InstanceIndex`, i.e. exactly the values naga produces for WGSL's `@builtin(vertex_index)`/`@builtin(instance_index)` (base instance included, which is what the wgpu v29 `shaderDrawParameters` fix and the 3,300-instance batching verification depend on). Measured, not assumed: `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.2/1.3` accepts Slang's SPIR-V (including the `DrawParameters` module), so the SPIR-V was always valid and only the readers were the limit; two standalone probes (`tmp/wgpu-probe`, `tmp/dawn-probe`) then established that naga accepts a SPIR-V vertex module using the Vulkan builtins **plus** a SPIR-V fragment module carrying a `PushConstant` block, creating a pipeline with `immediateSize = 16` and **0 errors**, while the `SV_VertexID` control still fails with `unsupported capability DrawParameters`. Slang has no SPIR-V version/profile knob (`slangc -h profile` lists only `sm_*`/`glsl_*`) and its glslang-mediated form still declares the capability, so the builtin choice was the only lever. | **Restored and re-verified.** `shader_cache.cpp` targets `SLANG_SPIRV` in both compile paths again, returns bytes (`std::vector<std::uint8_t>`) and `ShaderCache::get` wraps them as `ShaderCacheSource::SpirV`; the three builtin sites are renamed (`core_graph/src/plugin.cpp` fullscreen vertex, `sprite_render/src/mesh2d_pipeline.cpp` mesh2d instance index, `sprite_render/src/render.cpp` sprite). Verification: full build green; `ctest` **85/85 in 22.37 s**; the 58-example sweep is **57 clean** with only the pre-existing `fallingsand_falling_sand` material2d error; captures inspected — `mesh_batching` draws all 3,300 instances at distinct positions, `render_glfw_tonemapping` draws its eight tonemapping panels, `text_text_interactive` draws its four text lines inside their outlines, and `mesh_alpha_mask` draws the checker with its mask. **Two regressions surfaced only because SPIR-V pipelines become ready earlier than WGSL ones did, and both are fixed here:** (1) `ColorMaterial`'s bind group substituted the fallback image for a texture that was *declared but not yet prepared*, where Bevy's derive returns `AsBindGroupError::RetryNextUpdate` (`bevy_render/macros/src/as_bind_group.rs:568`), which permanently whitened `mesh_alpha_mask` — `color_material.hpp` now returns `RetryBindGroupNextUpdate` for that case exactly as the derive does; (2) `text_interactive` measured its bounds and ink outlines from a top-left block origin while the Bevy-aligned `Text2d` anchors the block through its required `Anchor` component (default `CENTER`), so the example now spawns its text with `Anchor::TOP_LEFT` and the text and outlines agree. |
| 2026-10 | **Backend investigation recorded: Dawn is not a SPIR-V escape hatch, and neither is a newer Slang.** Dawn's prebuilt 2026-09-18 Windows x64 release was downloaded, wired into a standalone probe through its own CMake package, and run against the same 5070 Ti Vulkan adapter; it rejects the capability outright with `Error while parsing SPIR-V: SPIR-V capability 'DrawParameters' is not supported` (measured with the glslang-rendered form of the same Slang shader, because the direct Slang SPIR-V failed even earlier as unparsable — Tint's own SPIR-V is accepted by the same probe, so the probe is sound). Dawn **does** implement immediates — its `webgpu.h` declares `WGPUPipelineLayoutDescriptor.immediateSize`, `WGPULimits.maxImmediateSize`, `wgpuRenderPassEncoderSetImmediates` and `WGPUWGSLLanguageFeatureName_ImmediateAddressSpace`, which corrects an earlier claim in this log that push constants were wgpu-native-only — but it has no `InstanceExtras`, `NativeFeature` or log-callback equivalents, and in this environment it could only load DLLs that sat next to the exe (`DynamicLib.Open: vulkan-1.dll Windows Error: 87`, likewise `d3dcompiler_47.dll`/`dxcompiler.dll`). Slang cannot carry WGSL immediates either: its master WGSL emitter contains no `immediate` handling and lowers push constants with a single `var<uniform>` emission, and `var<immediate>` appears **0** times in `slang-compiler.dll`. | **Dawn rejected as a backend; wgpu-native plus SPIR-V retained.** The capability matrix this leaves behind is what makes the WGSL workaround unnecessary: naga accepts SPIR-V except `DrawParameters`, and accepts a fragment `PushConstant` block once the device requests `WGPUNativeFeature_Immediates` **and** a non-zero `maxImmediateSize` (the adapter reports 256, and the Functionality path already copies adapter limits/features); Tint/Dawn rejects `DrawParameters`; Slang's SPIR-V is valid per `spirv-val`. S11 (`Wireframe2dPlugin`) can therefore use Bevy's real 16-byte push-constant colour on the SPIR-V path, and the per-shader target override that was considered for it is unnecessary. |
| 2026-10 | **2D text was rebuilt on Bevy's own text render path, which also fixes the invisible-text regression that followed the Slang-to-WGSL migration (the old path queued and prepared its items and had a ready pipeline, yet never issued a draw -- that failure was never localised further and is moot now): the glyph atlas is one `TextureDimension::D2` image plus a `TextureAtlasLayout`, and the glyphs are drawn by `SpritePipeline`, not by a text-specific pipeline.** The Bevy evidence was read line by line before any edit: `bevy_sprite_render/src/text2d/mod.rs:25-273` (`extract_text2d_sprite`) turns each glyph into one `ExtractedSlice { offset, rect, size }` and each run of consecutive glyphs sharing an atlas texture into one `ExtractedSprite { kind: Slices { indices }, image_handle_id: atlas_info.texture }`; `queue_sprites` (`render/mod.rs:471-561`) queues those sprites against `RenderVisibleEntities::iter::<Sprite>()` and the shared `DrawSprite = (SetItemPipeline, SetSpriteViewBindGroup<0>, SetSpriteTextureBindGroup<1>, DrawSpriteBatch)` command list (`render/mod.rs:838-843`); `crates/bevy_text` contains **no** pipeline, bind group, WGSL or even a glyphon dependency -- the atlas is an ordinary `Image` asset sampled through the sprite pipeline's hand-written `sprite_material_layout` (`texture_2d` + `sampler`, `render/mod.rs:69-78`, `sprite.wgsl:51-52`). The atlas itself is `Image::new_fill(size, TextureDimension::D2, &[0,0,0,0], Rgba8UnormSrgb, MAIN_WORLD | RENDER_WORLD)` plus `TextureAtlasLayout::new_empty(size)` and `DynamicTextureAtlasBuilder::new(size, 1)` (`font_atlas.rs:34-59`), where `size` is a square `max(1 << (32 - glyph_max_size.leading_zeros()), 512)` and overflow appends a second `FontAtlas` to the key's `Vec` (`font_atlas.rs:126-182`); a glyph's quad is the atlas rect itself and its `position` is the quad **center** in the block's +Y-down space, `x = glyph_size.x / 2 + location.offset.x + physical_glyph.x`, `y = line_y.round() + physical_glyph.y - location.offset.y + glyph_size.y / 2` (`pipeline.rs:441-453`), which the renderer negates (`text2d/mod.rs:248`); `FontSmoothing::None` thresholds the bitmap alpha at 50% and asks for a nearest sampler (`font_atlas.rs:48-50,212-225`); `Text2d` requires `Anchor`, `Visibility`, `VisibilityClass` and `Transform` and registers the **Sprite** visibility class through `on_add = visibility::add_visibility_class::<Sprite>` (`bevy_sprite/src/text2d.rs:82-96`), which is why the sprite queue finds text without a `Sprite` component; and `calculate_bounds_text2d` inserts the block's `Aabb` (`text2d.rs:316-347`). Epix instead had a `Texture2DArray` atlas with one layer per glyph, a bespoke three-bind-group `text-` pipeline with its own embedded Slang shaders and an `SV_VulkanInstanceID` instance index, a generated `TextMesh` per text entity and a `TextImage` component -- exactly the divergence this row closes. | **Rewritten, and the previously invisible text now renders.** `font.hpp`/`font.cpp`: `AtlasRect`, `apply_pending` and the layer packing are gone, replaced by `GlyphAtlasLocation`, `GlyphAtlasInfo`, `TextError` and `FontAtlas { dynamic_texture_atlas_builder, glyph_to_atlas_index, texture_atlas, texture }`, with `FontAtlasSet` keyed to `std::vector<FontAtlas>` and `add_glyph_to_atlas` implementing Bevy's try-every-atlas-then-create rule, its `max(1 << (32 - countl_zero(max_bitmap_dim)), 512)` atlas size, the `Rgba8UnormSrgb` format, the 1-pixel atlas padding and the `FontSmoothing::None` alpha threshold; FreeType bitmaps are now blitted top-row-first, because the old row flip served the y-up array atlas while Bevy's slice UVs require the image order FreeType already uses. `text.hpp`/`text.cpp`: `GlyphInfo` gains Bevy's `position`, `size` and `atlas_info`, `ShapedText` gains `size()` (Bevy's rounded advance box, `pipeline.rs:356-358,484`), `shape_text` takes the atlas set plus both asset stores because Bevy places glyphs during layout (`pipeline.rs:419-453`), and the `regen_mesh_for_shaped_text` system that existed only to build the mesh is deleted. `render.hpp`/`render.cpp`: `TextMesh`, `TextImage`, the text pipeline cache, its shaders, its instance buffer and its bind-group layouts are deleted; `Text2d` now requires `Anchor`/`Visibility`/`VisibilityClass` and registers the `Sprite` class through an `on_add` hook; `extract_text2d_sprite` is registered in the render world's `ExtractSchedule` **after** `SpriteSystems::ExtractSprites` (Bevy registers it `.after(SpriteSystems::ExtractSprites)`, `lib.rs:104-105`, because sprite extraction clears both extracted stores); `calculate_bounds_text2d` mirrors Bevy's AABB formula; and the per-entity geometry is factored into `detail::extract_text2d_layout` so the emitted slices are directly testable. `text/CMakeLists.txt` now links `epix_sprite`, `epix_sprite_render` and `epix_core_graph` (text depends on the sprite modules, just as `bevy_sprite_render`'s text2d support depends on `bevy_text`), and `text/modules/mod.cppm` imports them. The obsolete `tests/*/render_embedded_shader_loading.cpp` (the text shaders no longer exist) is replaced by `tests/*/text_render_path.cpp`. **Verification:** full no-modules build green; `ctest` **85/85 in 22.39 s**; the eight new text tests pass -- `Text2d.RegistersTheSpriteVisibilityClassAndItsRequiredComponents` (the `on_add` hook really does put the `Sprite` TypeId into `VisibilityClass`), `Text2dExtraction.EmitsOneSlicePerGlyphAndGroupsThemIntoSpriteRuns` (one slice per glyph, `offset == (position.x, -position.y)`, `size == rect.size()`, a single `Slices` sprite covering the whole run, and the centered-anchor top-left at `(-w/2, +h/2)`), `Text2dExtraction.TextBoundsOverrideTheLayoutSizeForAnchoring`, `FontAtlas.UsesASingleD2ImageWithAtlasLayoutRects` (one image, `depth_or_layers == 1`, `size == layout size >= 512`, every glyph rect inside it with non-zero alpha blitted in), `FontAtlas.NonAntialiasedGlyphsAreThresholdedAndSampledNearest` (every alpha byte is 0 or 255 and the sampler is nearest), plus the three shape/layout tests. **Rendered evidence:** `examples_header_text_text_interactive` now draws `Hello, Epix Engine! Interactive text test.` over four lines (capture inspected; the same binary previously drew nothing at all) and `examples_header_text_font_image` draws all three of its text lines, both with **0 stderr bytes**. |
| 2026-10 | **Text-path divergences that remain after the rewrite, recorded so a later reader does not mistake them for oversights.** The render path itself is now Bevy's -- sprite-pipeline draw, `Image`-asset atlas with `TextureAtlasLayout` rects, quad-center positions in +Y-down layout space, `Slices` sprites, the Sprite visibility class, `calculate_bounds_text2d` -- so what is listed here is either an Epix-internal choice or a Bevy feature Epix does not implement yet. **(1) Rasterizer and shaper:** FreeType plus HarfBuzz replace cosmic-text/swash; the interchange format is preserved (the same white-RGB, coverage-alpha, top-row-first `Rgba8UnormSrgb` bitmap with swash-compatible `(left, top)` placement), so `add_glyph_to_atlas`, the atlas geometry and the slice maths are the same algorithm. **(2) System location:** `extract_text2d_sprite` lives in `epix::text` rather than in `bevy_sprite_render` behind a `bevy_text` feature, because Epix's dependency direction is text to sprite-render; ordering and emitted data are Bevy's. **(3) Atlas key:** `FontAtlasKey { size, anti_aliased }` instead of `(AssetId<Font>, font_size.to_bits(), FontSmoothing)`, since an Epix `FontAtlasSet` already belongs to one font asset and `anti_aliased` is the boolean form of `FontSmoothing::{None, AntiAliased}`; subpixel smoothing is absent from both (Bevy marks it a TODO). **(4) Not implemented:** text spans and per-span colours (`TextSpan`, `ComputedTextBlock`; Epix has one span per entity, so sprite grouping keys on the atlas texture alone), `Text2dShadow`, `Underline`/`Strikethrough`/`TextBackgroundColor` decorations, `FontHinting`, a `LineHeight` component (Epix keeps `line_height` on `TextFont`) and `TextLayoutInfo::scale_factor` (Epix has no DPI scale factor, hence no `* GlobalTransform::from_scale(scale_factor.recip())` term in the extractor). **(5) Epix extensions kept:** `Text2d::offset` and the `TextMeasure` component. **(6) Atlas lifetime:** Bevy's `free_unused_font_atlases_system` (`font_atlas_set.rs`) has no counterpart; Epix drops a font's atlases with the font asset. **(7) Limits:** Bevy's `FontAtlas::new` ignores `max_texture_dimension_2d` when it picks the atlas size and Epix follows it deliberately, so a glyph larger than the device limit would fail at texture creation exactly as in Bevy. **(8) The `InstanceExtras`-chain crash and the inert `WGPU_BACKEND`/`WGPU_VALIDATION` recorded in the v29 row are unchanged by this work.** | **Out of the audited module set, documented rather than fixed.** This audit covers `mesh`, `image`, `sprite` and `core_graph`; | 2026-10 | **The Slang-to-WGSL migration landed and the mesh2d path is now correct end to end; two regressions remain, one of them pre-existing.** `shader_cache.cpp`'s two compile paths now target `SLANG_WGSL` and return the emitted text as a `std::string`, and `ShaderCache::get` wraps it as `ShaderCacheSource::Wgsl`, so naga's native frontend parses the engine's shaders instead of the SPIR-V it rejects. Verified on the v29 tree: the whole no-modules tree builds green; **ctest 85/85 in 53.55 s**; the 58-example sweep reports **57 clean with a single flagged example** (`fallingsand_falling_sand`, whose first error is the unrelated CPU-side `A specialized mesh pipeline must produce one vertex buffer.`), where the same sweep previously flagged every mesh-using example with device invalidation; and the captures are now correct rather than merely error-free. | **The evidence a screenshot cannot fake:** `examples_header_mesh_rendering` draws all five meshes at their own transforms -- coral rectangle left of centre, gradient quad at the origin, blue circle overlapping the gold rectangle on the right, rainbow quad below -- matching the example's `(-300,0)`, `(0,0)`, `(250,36)`, `(300,-18)` and `(-120,-220)`; `examples_header_mesh_batching` draws all **3,300** instances (3,000 opaque + 300 transparent) at distinct positions, which is the proof that `@builtin(instance_index)` now includes the base instance (the v29 `shaderDrawParameters` fix, the reason for the upgrade); `mesh_alpha_mask` is unchanged; and every capture has empty stderr. **Two regressions recorded, and the first is new:** (1) **the `InstanceExtras` chain crashes `wgpuCreateInstance` on v29** -- bisected by chaining it with *no* field setters and watching the example die with `0xC0000005` while the identical build without the chain runs -- so `backends`, `flags`, the DX12 compiler and the GLES minor version cannot reach the instance yet and `WGPU_BACKEND`/`WGPU_VALIDATION` are inert until that is fixed; (2) **text renders no glyphs** (`text_text_interactive` draws only the example's debug bounds, `text_font_image` is blank). The text failure is *not* caused by the migration: `extract texts`, `queue texts` and `prepare text batches` all run every frame, the `text-RGBA8UnormSrgb` pipeline is created successfully with no errors, and the runtime trace shows **no `text-*` pipeline is ever set for a draw** -- so nothing is being queued into `Transparent2D` for text, while the same phase draws the mesh2d items. | 2026-10 | **The wgpu-native v29 bump: it compiles, it explains its failures far better than v25, and it cannot ingest Epix's Slang shaders -- which turns the upgrade into a shader-toolchain migration.** The bump (approved for its `shaderDrawParameters` fix) needed, and got, these migrations: push constants to immediates (`TrackedRenderPass::set_push_constants` now calls `setImmediates`, and `LayoutCache` reduces Bevy's range list to the layout's single `immediateSize`), `wgpu::PushConstantRange` replaced by an Epix `PushConstantRange` because the headers removed the type, `ProgrammableStageDescriptor` to `ComputeState` (11 sites), SPIR-V input moved to a chained `ShaderSourceSPIRV` on the ordinary descriptor, the `ShaderSourceSPIRV` **instance feature**, `NativeFeature::eImmediates`, the two texture-format renames (`eRg16Unorm`/`eRgba16Unorm` to `eRG16Unorm`/`eRGBA16Unorm`, 15 sites -- they came from v25's *native* enum and are core formats now), a tracked patch for the pinned imgui backend's three header-generation fixes, and a generator fix so a `to_string` switch drops duplicate enum values (v29 dropped DX11, collapsing `InstanceBackend::Secondary` onto `_GL`). After that the tree built green, and then failed at runtime in a way v25 never could have explained: `wgpuCreateInstance` crashed with the `InstanceExtras` chain (bisected: without the chain the instance and adapter succeed), the device request was refused with `Some experimental features, EXPERIMENTAL_RAY_QUERY | EXPERIMENTAL_COOPERATIVE_MATRIX, were requested, but experimental features are not enabled` (Bevy's Functionality priority takes every adapter feature; the runtime exposes no switch, so they are no longer auto-requested), and finally `In wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModule: unsupported capability DrawParameters`. That last one is the blocker: v29 validates SPIR-V through naga, and `slangc` declares the `DrawParameters` capability for **every** vertex shader -- proved by compiling a vertex-only probe (`SV_VertexID`, no instance builtin) and reading the assembly: `OpCapability DrawParameters` with `BuiltIn BaseVertex` and `BuiltIn VertexIndex` decorations. | **Consequence: the "naga path" means WGSL, not SPIR-V-through-naga, so the upgrade is a shader-toolchain migration.** The route is proven feasible: `slangc -target wgsl` was run over a representative engine-shaped shader and its output preserved everything the port relies on -- `[[vk::binding(b, g)]]` became `@binding(b) @group(g)`, `[[vk::location(n)]]` became `@location(n)`, `SV_VertexID`/`SV_InstanceID` became `@builtin(vertex_index)`/`@builtin(instance_index)` with no capability at all, and the storage layouts were emitted with explicit `@align(16)` array-of-`vec4` packing. **Decision (user): stay on v29 and migrate Slang output to WGSL as a temporary measure, with SPIR-V kept as the preferred path for when naga supports the DrawParameters capability.** The migration is contained in `shader_cache.cpp` (both compile paths: the target descriptor plus a `std::string` return instead of SPIR-V bytes, and the cache-entry construction that currently wraps the result as `ShaderCacheSource::SpirV`), but it also touches the tests that assert on SPIR-V payloads, so it is its own round of work; the v29 tree is currently work-in-progress and uncommitted. Two other v29 findings worth keeping: its error text is qualitatively better than v25's (it named the experimental features, the unsupported capability and the shader-module failure where v25 produced only "Parent device is lost"), and the still-open question of whether Slang's WGSL backend covers the *whole* shader corpus (atomics, modules/imports, texture types and the Slang `#define`/import machinery are untested; only representative shaders were probed). |
| 2026-10 | **The all-meshes-at-one-position bug: the packed mesh2d matrix was decoded with the wrong layout, and, behind that, wgpu's Vulkan backend cannot report a base instance at all.** With the entry-point fix in place the example rendered (square, circle and gradient quad) but every mesh drew at the same point. Three measurements localised it: (1) a temporary probe in `extract_mesh2d` printed the stored world translations as `(-300,0,0)`, `(0,0,0)`, `(250,36,0.1)`, `(300,-18,0)`, `(-120,-220,0)` -- extraction was correct; (2) the runtime's own trace showed the draw calls carrying distinct ranges -- `RenderPass::draw_indexed 6 1 0 0 0/1/2/3` -- so the batching was correct; (3) the fault was therefore in the packed matrix. `Mesh2dUniform::from_components` packs Bevy's layout exactly (three `Vec4` rows, each row's `w` holding that row's translation component), but the shader declared the member as `column_major float4x3` and decoded it with matrix indexing (`world_from_local[3][r]` as the translation). A Slang `float4x3` is a four-column matrix, so that read landed in the following struct member (`local_from_world_transpose_a`, identity for an untransformed mesh, hence `(1,0,0)`) and every mesh was drawn at the same place near the origin. | **The decoding is fixed and verified.** The packed data is now declared in the shader as explicit arrays -- `float4 world_from_local[3]` and `float4 local_from_world_transpose_a[2]`, the same shape as Bevy's Rust-side `[Vec4; 3]`/`[Vec4; 2]` -- and `mesh2d_position_local_to_world`, `mesh2d_normal_local_to_world` (Bevy's `mat2x4_f32_to_mat3x3_unpack`) and `mesh2d_tangent_local_to_world` are written as dot products over those rows, which is precisely what Bevy's `affine3_to_square` plus matrix-vector multiply computes. The capture confirms it: the stack of meshes moved from the screen centre to `(-300, 0)`, i.e. instance 0's real translation. **What remains is a separate, external cause, and the instance data is not at fault:** every draw still reads instance 0. `wgpu-hal`'s Vulkan backend never enables `VkPhysicalDeviceShaderDrawParametersFeatures` -- there is no mention of it anywhere in its Vulkan code in the v25 sources, and the same is true of v26 and v27 (both fetched and checked); only wgpu trunk adds it (`adapter.rs`, `shader_draw_parameters` for API >= 1.1). Since Slang's `SV_InstanceID` lowers to SPIR-V `InstanceIndex`, a builtin gated behind the `DrawParameters` capability, the runtime silently ignores the base instance, `mesh[instance_index]` reads element 0 for every draw, and `VUID-VkShaderModuleCreateInfo-pCode-08740` fires once per affected module. Epix's sprite path is immune because it fetches its transform from per-instance **vertex attributes** (`i_model_transpose_col0..2` in `sprite_render/src/render.cpp`), where instance stepping happens in the hardware vertex fetch. This also rules out the v27 upgrade as a remedy for it. Options, pending the user's decision: enable the feature by building/patched-running the runtime (the upstream fix, which would keep the engine and shaders Bevy-shaped), or avoid the builtin inside Epix (Bevy's dynamic-offset uniform path, or passing the batch's instance base explicitly) and record the divergence. |

In Epix both are initialized before either runs, so the dependent system's `Res<Mesh2dPipeline>` validation fails, it never runs, `Material2dPipeline<M>` is never created, and every `Render` system reading it stays permanently disabled — the `initialize_systems(force=false)` model means a failed validation is never retried, which is why the frame stays black on every frame rather than only the first. **Corrected mechanism (2026-10, after two reverted attempts).** The initialization framing above describes the symptom, not the cause. The actual mechanism is deferred commands: `DeferredApply::ApplyEnd` is the default (`schedule.hpp:230-238`), so `init_mesh_2d_pipeline`'s `commands.insert_resource(Mesh2dPipeline{...})` stays queued until the schedule ends, and `init_material_2d_pipeline` cannot observe the resource during the same run **regardless of how the two systems are ordered or when they are initialized**. Bevy works because its scheduler auto-inserts `apply_deferred` sync points between systems whose commands a later system observes; Epix has `apply_deferred` on `System`/`Schedule` and a `DeferredApply` policy, but no automatic sync-point insertion. My first attempt put `initialize` inline in the dispatch lambdas — including the parallel multithread/flat paths and the taskflow tasks — which is unsafe for exactly the reason above; those edits were fully reverted (`git checkout`), so the executors are byte-identical to HEAD. Not started. |

| 2026-10 | **C19 read in full (785 lines of Rust + 338 lines of WGSL) and staged, because the port has more surface than one round can verify.** Structure to port, all from `bevy_core_pipeline/src/experimental/mip_generation/mod.rs`: the `DownsampleDepthShader` resource; `DEPTH_PYRAMID_MIP_COUNT = 12`; `DownsampleDepthPipeline`/`DownsampleDepthPipelines` (four pipelines: first/second x standard/multisample, plus one shared non-filtering sampler); `create_downsample_depth_bind_group_layout` (14 entries: the depth texture, twelve `R32Float` storage textures - binding 6 is `ReadWrite` because the second phase reads mip 6 while writing mip 7 - and the sampler); `DownsampleDepthPipelineKey` (`MULTISAMPLE`, `SECOND_PHASE`) with entry points `downsample_depth_first`/`downsample_depth_second` and a `0..4` compute push constant carrying the mip count; the dummy `R32Float` texture created in `RenderStartup` and used for unused mip slots; `ViewDepthPyramid { all_mips, mips[12], mip_count }` built from `TextureCache` with `depth_pyramid_size = (size + 1) / 2` and `mip_count = depth_pyramid_size.max_mips(D2)`; `ViewDownsampleDepthBindGroup`; `prepare_view_depth_pyramids` and `prepare_downsample_depth_view_bind_groups` (after `prepare_core_3d_depth_textures`, `run_if(resource_exists::<DownsampleDepthPipelines>)`); and `DownsampleDepthNode`, registered twice (at `EarlyDownsampleDepth` and `LateDownsampleDepth`) with the graph edges `(EarlyPrepass, EarlyDeferredPrepass, EarlyDownsampleDepth, LatePrepass, LateDeferredPrepass)` and `(StartMainPassPostProcessing, LateDownsampleDepth, EndMainPassPostProcessing)`. The dispatch itself is two compute passes over the shared bind group: the first over `ceil(view_size / 64)` workgroups, then, only when `mip_count >= 7`, the second over one workgroup. **Feasibility confirmed, and one of my own claims corrected again:** a `Select-String` scan said Epix dispatches no compute pass anywhere, but that scan used the non-recursing `**` glob once more - `experimental/examples/{header,module}/{voxel_path_tracer,brickmap_path_tracer,liquid_eulerian}.cpp` call `get_compute_pipeline` and dispatch, `render/render/tests/header/render_smoke_test.cpp:83-91` queues a compute pipeline and asserts it compiled, and `experimental/examples/module/liquid_eulerian.cpp:1967` does the same, so Epix's compute path is exercised by examples that pass in the sweep. `TextureCache::get(device, key, descriptor)` -> `CachedTexture{texture, default_view}` and `GpuReadback` are both available for the verification path. **Staged plan, in the order the risk sits:** (1) the Slang port of `downsample_depth` plus the pyramid layout/pipeline/dummy-texture machinery and its tests, including a GPU test that queues both entry points and asserts they compile (this is the step that validates the risky surface: twelve storage-texture bindings, a `ReadWrite` binding, compute push constants, `groupshared` reduction, and the MSAA variant, whose HLSL/Slang form needs a compile-time sample count where WGSL's `texture_depth_multisampled_2d` is count-agnostic - so the multisample pipeline key must carry the sample count as a shader def); (2) the node, the two `prepare_*` systems, the `CoreGraphPlugin` hookup and a GPU correctness test that dispatches on a synthetic depth texture and reads the smallest mip back to prove the min-reduction; (3) the early/late prepass consumers, i.e. the culling shader inside `batching/gpu_preprocessing.rs` plus the indirect-parameter readback, and only then a visual example, because nothing samples the pyramid until culling exists. Step 1 is ordinary graphics work on infrastructure Epix already uses (the path tracers); step 3's last link is again the missing 3D mesh renderer. **Three concrete translation risks to settle while doing step 1, all of which only surface at pipeline creation or dispatch time (hence the compile-the-pipelines test comes first):** (a) *write-only storage images* - Bevy's WGSL declares `texture_storage_2d<r32float, write>` for eleven of the twelve mip bindings and the layout uses `StorageTextureAccess::WriteOnly`, but Slang/HLSL has no write-only storage-texture declaration (`RWTexture2D` is read-write, which naga compiles without the `NonReadable` decoration wgpu expects for a `WriteOnly` layout entry), so either those layout entries become `ReadWrite` as a documented Slang necessity or the shader needs an explicit decoration; (b) *the multisample variant* - `texture_depth_multisampled_2d` is sample-count agnostic and the WGSL uses `textureNumSamples`, while an HLSL/Slang `Texture2DMS<T, N>` needs a compile-time `N`, so `DownsampleDepthPipelineKey` must carry the sample count as a shader def rather than only `MULTISAMPLE`; (c) *twelve storage textures in one bind group* exceed Vulkan's guaranteed `maxPerStageDescriptorStorageImages` of 4, so the port must keep Bevy's `is_culling_supported()` gate (which is what makes the pipelines conditional in the first place) and report a clear diagnostic when the device cannot provide them - `CreateComputePipelineError`/`PipelineServer` already surface pipeline failures, but the pyramid path should not silently produce a pass that never runs. Also worth noting for the port: Epix's `ComputePipelineDescriptor::layouts` holds `BindGroupLayoutDescriptor`s (matching Bevy) even though its `add_layout` setter takes a `wgpu::BindGroupLayout`, and `SpecializedComputePipelines<S>::specialize` takes a `const PipelineServer&` where Bevy passes a `PipelineCache`. **Landed 2026-10 (part 1 of 2):** `core_graph/include/epix/core_graph/mip_generation.hpp` and `src/mip_generation.cpp` now carry `DEPTH_PYRAMID_MIP_COUNT = 12`, `depth_pyramid_size`, `max_mips_for_size`, `depth_pyramid_mip_count`, `DownsampleDepthPipelineKey` (with the hash Bevy's `SpecializedComputePipelines` needs), `DownsampleDepthPipeline::specialize` (entry points, `MULTISAMPLE` shader def, the `0..4` mip-count push constant), `create_downsample_depth_bind_group_layout` (all fourteen entries; the storage entries are read-write because Slang cannot declare a write-only storage image) and `create_depth_pyramid_dummy_texture`. Six new tests in `core_graph/tests/header/mip_generation_tests.cpp` pin the pyramid size and mip-count arithmetic against Bevy's `Extent3d::max_mips`, the key flags, the four specialized descriptors, and - on a real device - that both layouts and the dummy texture are accepted. **Landed 2026-10 (part 2a):** the faithful Slang port of `downsample_depth.wgsl` (the FidelityFX SPD single-pass min-reduction, both phases, meshlet paths omitted since Epix has no meshlets), `DownsampleDepthShader`, `DownsampleDepthPipelines` and `create_downsample_depth_pipelines` (a `RenderStartup` system that runs once, gated on `is_culling_supported()`, creates both layouts, the shared non-filtering sampler and specializes Bevy's four pipelines), and `MipGenerationPlugin` — registered from `CoreGraphPlugin` exactly where Bevy's `CorePipelinePlugin` adds it (`bevy_core_pipeline/src/lib.rs:44-51`). A new test attaches the plugin, waits for the embedded shader to load, runs `RenderStartup` and asserts the plugin specialized all four keys, then pushes the four descriptors through the pipeline server and asserts each one *compiles* - which is what caught two real Slang errors (`RWTexture2D::Load` takes `int2`, not `int3`; `DepthTexture2DMS` takes only a format argument, not a sample count, so like WGSL's `texture_depth_multisampled_2d` the sample count is not part of the type and is read with `GetDimensions`). **Landed 2026-10 (part 2b):** `ViewDepthPyramid` (built from `TextureCache` with a `TextureCacheKey` mirroring the descriptor, per-mip views, and the dummy texture filling the mips a smaller depth buffer does not need), its `create_bind_group` and its two-dispatch `downsample_depth`; `ViewDownsampleDepthBindGroup`; `prepare_view_depth_pyramids` and `prepare_downsample_depth_view_bind_groups` (the latter picking the multisampled layout and accepting either a `ViewDepthTexture` or an `OcclusionCullingSubview` as the source); and `DownsampleDepthNode`, registered twice at `Core3dNodes::EarlyDownsampleDepth`/`LateDownsampleDepth` with Bevy's two edge chains via Epix's `RenderGraphExt` equivalents. The plugin now also matches Bevy's scheduling: the dummy texture in `RenderStartup`, `create_downsample_depth_pipelines` in `RenderSystems::Prepare` (it had been in `RenderStartup`), and the two prepare systems chained in `PrepareResources` after `prepare_core_3d_depth_textures`. Two C++-forced translations are recorded: Epix has no `resource_exists` run condition, so the prepare systems take their pipeline/dummy-texture resources as `std::optional<Res<T>>` and return early (same behaviour, Epix's mechanism); and Bevy's node is a plain `Node` with its own `QueryState`s because it also walks shadow-map subviews, while Epix's is a `ViewNodeRunner` view node that handles the main view - `OcclusionCullingSubview` has no producer in Epix yet, so the optional guard Bevy applies is always taken. Still not verified, and the last work on this row: a dispatch-and-readback test proving the SPD min-reduction actually produces the expected mip values. Historical note on the shader's binding risk: wgpu requires a depth-typed sampled binding for the view's depth texture, and HLSL/Slang has no depth-image type, so the shader must be settled empirically rather than assumed. **Resolved 2026-10: no divergence is needed.** The depth binding *is* expressible in Slang, and the earlier "hard blocker" reading was wrong twice over. First, the fatal `Invalid fragment shader module for render pipeline descriptor` from `wgpuDeviceCreateShaderModule` was not the depth operand at all: the module was demanding the `StorageImageReadWithoutFormat` capability because every write-only storage image lacked an explicit format. Slang's core-module reference is explicit that storage formats must be declared with the `[format("…")]` attribute, so each of the twelve pyramid storage bindings carries `[[vk::image_format("r32f")]]`. Second, the depth binding itself is a built-in type: Slang ships `DepthTexture2D`/`DepthTexture2DMS` (the `_Texture` specialization with `isShadow = 1`, "indicates whether the texture is a depth texture"), which the SPIR-V backend emits as a depth image - the fix [shader-slang/slang#8604](https://github.com/shader-slang/slang/pull/8604) added, as recorded in [gfx-rs/wgpu#7332](https://github.com/gfx-rs/wgpu/issues/7332), and both types are listed with SPIR-V support in the Slang docs. Hand-rolling the equivalent `_Texture<float, __Shape2D, 0, 0, 0, 0, 1, 0, 0>` typealias did *not* work (it still produced a storage image), which is why the built-in type is the form to use. Verified by a new test that queues a real compute pipeline with the device-validated layout and asserts it compiled: `MipGeneration.DepthTextureBindingCompilesWithSlangDepthType`. C19 part 2 can therefore port `downsample_depth.wgsl` faithfully, with no copy pass and no change to the depth format. |

Result: OIT's draw half is a shader-library function writing bindings declared by `bevy_pbr::mesh_view_bindings` under `OIT_ENABLED`, so it lands with the PBR/mesh-pipeline port; mip generation's only consumer is occlusion culling (Bevy's own module doc says so) and its node sits at `Node3d::EarlyDownsampleDepth`/`LateDownsampleDepth`; the skybox is standalone (its own bind group 0) and needs **no image-module change** - a later check showed that "needs `ImageType::Cube`" was itself wrong, because pinned 0.18 has no such variant at all (its `ImageType` is the loader enum) and Epix already honours the cube view dimension through `texture_view_descriptor` (`image.cpp:91-102`). **The wrong claim:** the first version of the C19 row asserted that "a tree-wide scan finds no `OcclusionCulling`... in Epix". That scan used `Select-String -Path epix_engine\**\*.hpp,epix_engine\**\*.cpp`, and a PowerShell `**` glob does not recurse in that position, so it silently matched nothing; the plain `grep` search then found **51** references covering `render/occlusion_culling.hpp` (all three components + plugin + extraction), `GpuPreprocessingSupport::is_culling_supported`, the `GpuOcclusionCullingWorkItemBuffers` late/indirect streams in `batching.hpp`, the culling-aware view configuration in `core3d.cpp`, and parity tests. The row is corrected in place with that correction recorded. **Lesson for this audit:** never state an absence from a shell glob - use the recursive search tool, and treat any "there is none of X" as a claim that needs one more confirmation before it enters the tracker. **C19 is therefore closer than the row first implied:** the graph extension points already exist (`Core3dNodes::EarlyPrepass`/`LatePrepass`/`EarlyDownsampleDepth`/`LateDownsampleDepth`, `core3d.hpp:102-134`), the compute-pipeline infrastructure exists (`ComputePipelineDescriptor`, `queue_compute_pipeline`, `SpecializedComputePipelines`), and the batching side of culling is already ported. What remains for the chain is the pyramid itself, the culling compute shader (`batching/mesh_preprocess.wgsl`), the culling node plus indirect-parameter readback, and a 3D mesh renderer whose meshes could be culled. **C19 plan (next):** `core_graph/include/epix/core_graph/mip_generation.hpp` + `src/mip_generation.cpp` (the `DownsampleDepthShader` resource, `DEPTH_PYRAMID_MIP_COUNT = 12`, `ViewDepthPyramid` with its `create_bind_group`/`downsample_depth`, the dummy texture, `MipGenerationPlugin` registering the nodes at both downsample labels) and a Slang port of `downsample_depth.wgsl`, with tests for the mip-count/size arithmetic, the bind-group layouts, the dummy-texture path and the graph registration. |
